# Changelog

[한국어](CHANGELOG.ko.md).

## Unreleased

- `CONTRACTS` of `packages/ts/tests/case-rules.ts` is a constant of its module and no export: no other file reads it, and knip of `make lint-ts` reported it as an unused export. `make lint-ts` passes its 8 steps.
- No piece of 6 lines or more of code exists twice, where `make duplication-check` reported 12 clones. In the Rust core, `Error::part` of `packages/rust/src/error.rs` gives the kind and the category of each failure of hyper itself, or the error of the library that it passes on, once, and `category`, `kind`, `status` and `source` read it, where each of them listed the variants; the module `values` gives the map of a value and the texts of a list to `manifest`, `render`, `steps` and `session`; `manifest` and `router` share `is_identifier`; `keep::replace` and the two constructors of `reads::Read` each share their common step. The tests `packages/rust/tests/contracts.rs` compare a result with `raises` of its case through one function `check`. The function `plain` of the Node test tree is in `packages/ts/tests/inputs.ts`, which `tests/scripts/build-id.test.mts` reads in place of its own copy. No rule changes: `make core-test` passes 53 tests, `make core-lint` and `make core-fmt-check` pass, `make hyper-test-node` passes 18 tests and `make recipe-run-node` passes.
- `config/rebuild.json` gives `scripts/recipe/legacy.mts` the class `policy` with the reason of the other parts of the recipe check: it gives the check the fixed case files of `tests/legacy` as case files of the spec, a comparison that no ecosystem tool performs. Before, `make rebuild-check` failed with `scripts/recipe/legacy.mts: the tool has no class in config/rebuild.json`; it passes.
- `config/layout.yml` declares `tests/legacy` among the directories of the root `tests/` (`layout-root-tests`): it holds the fixed copies of case files that the PHP, Node and Python runners read for the host code that does not call the Rust core yet, and that the recipe check reads as case files of the spec, so it holds cases of three servers and no test of one package. Before, `make layout-check` failed with `tests/legacy: error: layout-root-tests`; it passes.
- `Polyspec\Hyper\HyperValueError::status()` declares the return type `null`, where it declared `?int`: an error of the category `value` has no status (HY-132.2), so the method returns no integer, and the analysis of `make analyse-php` reported the unused `int` of the type (`return.unusedType`). `make analyse-php` reports no error.
- The case reader of the PHP runner leaves out the message of an observed failure when the field `raises` of the case states `"message": null` (HY-132.4), as the Node and Python readers do. `statedMessage` of `packages/php/tests/Support/CaseRules.php` tested the expected failure with `is_array`, while the reader holds the objects of a case as `stdClass`, so it never left a message out, and the case `shared_data_that_holds_a_context_fails` of `contracts/context.json` failed with `FIELD_DIFFERS raises` for the generated program and for the native extension. `make hyper-test-php` passes 41 tests in both runs and `make recipe-run-php` passes.
- The host code of the three servers is in the group directories of HYR-4 below the package places of H-1: `packages/php/src/<Group>/`, `packages/ts/src/<group>/` and `packages/python/src/polyspec/hyper/<group>/`, and `contracts/interfaces.json` names those paths with its `groups`. The unit `hyper-value-error` of the concern `app` is in the group `app` (`packages/php/src/App/HyperValueError.php`, `packages/python/src/polyspec/hyper/app/_hyper_value_error.py`). The section `select` of `tests/legacy/accept-before-s3b.json` names the members `accept.best` and `accept.parse`, which its steps call, since the servers have no `Accept.select`. Every manifest holds the version 0.0.40, the two `composer.json` require `"min-0-max/json-schema": "^2.6.0-p2"`, and the locks follow.
- The statement of the open error of an application, the kind `invalid_application`, is HY-106.21, where it was HY-106.18, and HY-106.18 is the statement that an application takes every public name from the entry of the package: the namespace `Polyspec\Hyper`, the package `@polyspec/hyper` and the package `polyspec.hyper`. HY-106.18 keeps the meaning that the release 0.0.40 gave it, because a statement keeps its id only while its meaning stays. `contracts/protocol.json`, the case labels of `contracts/manifest.json`, `contracts/interfaces.json`, `packages/rust/interface.json` and the comments of the Rust core cite HY-106.21 for the open error (G-3).
- The lock of the Rust core pins `polyspec-binding-core` at the end of the branch `v1/rust-core` of binding, one commit after the one that the previous entry pinned, which the scaffold and the contract check of that commit run with (CK-20).
- The interface contract declares the class `app` as host code (origin `host`, binding L-3), not a class of the core: `App` holds the handlers, the services, the functions and the hooks of the application, which are objects of its language that the core never holds, reads the files of the application and runs the steps of `PageRequest`, calling the handler that each step asks for (C-7), while the pages and the request of the core are the classes `pages` and `page_request`. The binding generator refuses the contract for now with `not generated (G-4): app: a host class unit is not generated yet`.
- The lock of the Rust core pins `polyspec-binding-core` at the end of the published branch of binding, whose contract check compares the commit of a Git pin with the commit of the running binding (CK-20); the scaffold of that commit writes the binding crates as they are.
- The PHP, Node and Python runners read the sections whose cases the S-3b changes gave a form that the host code runs only when it calls the core (C-7) from their fixed copies `tests/legacy/<name>-before-s3b.json`, the sections as they were before the S-3b case changes: 30 sections of 15 case files, 795 cases for Python and 789 for Node; the browser runner, the build runner and the tests of the core read them live, and `docs/features.md` lists the live sections and the sections of the fixed copies. With the fixtures of the servers built, the Python runner passes 1454 of 1455 cases and the Node runner 1446 of 1447, where they failed 425 and 439 cases; the one case that fails needs a run by a user other than root, who reads a file of mode 0.
- The recipe check knows the fixed copies of the S-3b case changes: a section of `contracts/interfaces.json` may have no runner when its `fixedCopy` names `<name>-before-s3b.json` of `tests/legacy`, the section as it was before those changes, which the host runners read until the commit that moves its rules to the core (C-7) makes it live again (HYR-7). The schema of the spec allows such a section, the check fails a `fixedCopy` that is no fixed copy of the file holding the section, takes the steps of a fixed copy from its live file, accepts the statements that S-3b retired in a fixed copy, and leaves the member check of a section that no runner reads to its copy; the reader of the Node registry reads the sections that `prefixed` of the Node runner leaves out or keeps alone.
- The PHP and Python servers fail the place of the context in shared data that already holds the member `context` with the error of the category `value` and the kind `shared_holds_context` (HY-124.8, HY-132), as the browser package and the Node server do: PHP throws `Polyspec\Hyper\HyperValueError`, an `InvalidArgumentException` with `kind()` and `status()`, where it threw a `LogicException`, and Python raises `HyperValueError` of `polyspec.hyper._hyper_value_error`, a `ValueError` with `kind` and `status`, where it raised a plain `ValueError`; both classes are internal until the servers raise every error of HY-132. The withContext steps of the PHP and Python runners compare the failure as `raises`, and their case readers leave out the message of a failure whose case states none, as the Node reader does (HY-132.4); before, they compared the key `fails`, which the contract no longer holds. `contracts/interfaces.json` lists the unit `hyper-value-error` of PHP and Python.
- HY-73.32 states the order of `reads.json`, whose bytes the build id hashes (HY-125.15): the routes in manifest order, each with `shared` and then `regions`; the manifest regions that have a template, the page region and the route regions in manifest order; `keys` before `each`; and the keys of `keys` in the byte order of their UTF-8 text. The Rust core writes the keys in that order, where it kept the order in which the templates read them. What changes against the earlier implementation, `routeReads` of the browser package with `JSON.stringify`, which `hyper-build-server` keeps until it calls the core (H-8): its keys follow the order in which the templates read them, and the keys that are integers come first, as ECMAScript orders the keys of an object, so two builds of templates that read the same paths in another order wrote other bytes. The cases `cases` and `routes` of `contracts/reads.json` hold their read paths in that order, and the core compares their text.
- HY-73.31 replaces HY-73.10 and states the key that an index with a number literal adds to a read path: the number text of json, which writes the number as ECMAScript `Number::toString` does (RQ-030 of json), such as `1.5`, `1e+21`, `1e-7` and `123456789012345680`, while a negative number is an operator on a literal and reads its object whole (HY-73.11). The Rust core takes that text from `number_text` of polyspec-json, the one float-to-text function of polyspec, where it formatted the number itself and wrote `1e21` as `1000000000000000000000` and `1e-7` as `0.0000001`. Four cases of `contracts/reads.json` state a fraction, both exponent notations, a negative number and an integer beyond 2^53; `templateReads` of the browser package gives the same read paths for them as the core.
- The browser package fails `withContext` for shared data that already holds the member `context` with an error of the class `RangeError` of the category `value` that gives the kind `shared_holds_context` and no status, as HY-124.8 and HY-132 state; before, it threw an `Error` without a kind, and its message had the prefix `hyper: `. The step of the section `withContext` of `contracts/context.json`, which the browser runner and the Node runner share, compares the failure as `raises` with its kind, status and category, and the case reader of the Node test tree leaves out the message of a failure whose case states none (HY-132.4); before, the step compared the key `fails`, which the contract no longer holds, and the seven cases of the section failed for both runners.
- The lock of the Rust core pins `polyspec-binding-core` at the end of the branch of binding, the commit whose scaffold writes the binding crates, and the scaffold of that commit names the Python module `polyspec.hyper._binding._native` in `packages/python/native/src/lib.rs`. Its contract check reports CK-19 also for the packages of the Node addon per platform in `optionalDependencies` of `packages/ts/package.json` (binding T-2), which `docs/features.md` lists with the other entries that wait for the generated bindings.
- The Rust core gives the reserved template of the embedded data (H-7, H-8 step 0): HY-31.9 states that one rule of the core gives the name and the source of `hyper/data.tpl`, so that the build, which writes no copy of it below `program/templates/`, and every server take them from one place; `render::data_template` returns them as the record `data_template` of the interface contract. The section `dataTemplate` of `contracts/build.json` holds them, and the build runner, which reads `packages/client/data-template.json` as the command tool does, and the core give the same name and source.
- The Rust core computes the read paths (H-7, H-8 step 0): HY-73.30 states that one rule of the core computes the read paths of every route from the manifest and the template sources, in the form of the routes of `reads.json`, and those of one template; `read_paths::route_reads` and `read_paths::template_reads` implement HY-73.2 to HY-73.20 on the template AST of the template language, and the interface contract lists them. The section `routes` of `contracts/reads.json` holds the read paths of three applications, the board example among them, which the browser runner computes with `routeReads` of `packages/client/src/reads.ts`; the core gives the same read paths for them, in the same order of keys, and the same read paths as `templateReads` for the 24 cases of the section `cases`, and the pages of the core read its value. The command tool and the Node server keep the browser package until they call the core (H-8).
- The Rust core holds the template check of HY-48.3 (H-7, H-8 step 0): HY-48.13 states that one rule of the core gives every problem as a line that names its template, in the order of the `hx-*` attributes, the placements of the layout and the route regions of each route, and `template_rules::problems` gives them for the bytes of the manifest and the sources of the templates, which the interface contract lists as `template_problems`. The section `templateChecks` of `contracts/manifest.json` holds 13 cases, among them the regions fixture of the script tests, the placements of `{# build}`, `{# title}` and `{# data}`, `hx-*` attributes with their lines and route regions placed through included and placed templates; the build runner gives each of them to `packages/cli/lib/template-rules.mjs` and the core gives the same problems. The command tool and `make templates-check` keep the earlier implementation until the command tool calls the core (H-8).
- The Rust core holds the build id (H-7, H-8 step 0): HY-125.15 to HY-125.20, which replace HY-125.1, HY-125.2 and HY-125.4, state that the id covers every file that one build writes without the id, the files below `public/assets` that `client/assets.json` lists, the files below `client/` and `program/` and the `--static` files of the static shell, that the hash leaves out `client/build.json`, `program/build.json`, `csr/index.html` and the copies below `csr/assets`, that each path carries the prefix of its place and that a path without one or given twice fails the build with the kind `invalid_build`, that `build.json` of `program/` and `client/` holds the id, and that one rule checks the form of a build id; HYC-22.9 replaces HYC-22.6, because the page holds the outputs of another build. `build::id`, `build::check_id`, `build::file_text` and `build::read` of the core implement them with the error `InvalidBuild` (kind `invalid_build`), and the interface contract lists them with the record `build_output`. The build cases of `contracts/context.json` take the paths of the places and a case with a server output and a static file; the build runner, which still runs `packages/cli/lib/build-id.mjs`, and the core give the same order, stream and id for each of them, and the core reads the cases of `contracts/build.json` with the same results. The command tool keeps the earlier input until it calls the core (H-8).
- HY-125.14 states that the element name `hyper-build` is the name of the build id element and has nothing to do with the package names, so the renamed packages leave it as it is, and `docs/features.md` lists the native modules of the host packages with the four entries of CK-19 that wait for the generated bindings and the built extension (H-1a, H-1b).
- The packages take the names and the places of the plan items H-1 to H-3: the server library is the package of the main specification and carries the name of the repository, so the Rust core is the crate `polyspec-hyper` with the library `polyspec_hyper` in `packages/rust`, the PHP package the Composer package `polyspec/hyper` with the namespace `Polyspec\Hyper` in `packages/php`, the Python package the distribution `polyspec-hyper` with the module `polyspec.hyper` in `packages/python`, and the Node package the npm package `@polyspec/hyper` in `packages/ts`; `server` stays the role of the specification and no package name (HY-54.22), and the description of every manifest says first that it is the server library. Before 0.0.7 `@polyspec/hyper` was the browser package, which is `@polyspec/hyper-client` since. The build package `@polyspec/hyper-build` is the command tool `@polyspec/hyper-cli` in `packages/cli`; it keeps its two bins `hyper-build-assets` and `hyper-build-server` until H-8 makes them `hyper build` of the bin `hyper`. The names that change against 0.0.39 and the commits of this branch are: Composer `polyspec/hyper-server` to `polyspec/hyper`, PyPI `polyspec-hyper-server` to `polyspec-hyper` (wheel `polyspec_hyper`), npm `@polyspec/hyper-server` to `@polyspec/hyper` and `@polyspec/hyper-build` to `@polyspec/hyper-cli`, the crate `polyspec-hyper-server` to `polyspec-hyper` and its library to `polyspec_hyper`, the PHP namespace `Polyspec\Hyper\Server` and the Python module `polyspec.hyper.server` of the earlier commits back to `Polyspec\Hyper` and `polyspec.hyper`, with `create_server` imported from `polyspec.hyper`, the board namespace back to `Polyspec\Hyper\Examples\Board`, the products of `config/release.json` `hyper-server` to `hyper` and `hyper-build` to `hyper-cli`, so that the release assets are `polyspec-hyper-ts-X.Y.Z`, `polyspec-hyper-php-X.Y.Z`, `polyspec-hyper-python-X.Y.Z` and `polyspec-hyper-cli-ts-X.Y.Z`, and the module `client` of the core to `client_rendering`, so that `client` names only the browser package. The native modules sit in `native/` of their packages (binding L-2a), as the binding scaffold at the end of its branch writes them: the PHP extension `polyspec_hyper` in place of `polyspec_hyper_server` is the Composer package `polyspec/hyper-native` (type `php-ext`, crate `polyspec-hyper-php-ext`) in `packages/php/native`, without a `php-ext/` directory, the Python module `polyspec.hyper._binding._native` in place of `polyspec.hyper.server._native` comes from the crate `polyspec-hyper-python` in `packages/python/native`, and the Node addon `hyper.<target>.node` from the crate `polyspec-hyper-node` in `packages/ts/native`. The interface contract names the library hyper and records the package names and the native modules as changes, and its function types state `async_allowed`. HR-98 states the new layout, and `tests/scripts/package-names.test.mts` checks the manifest of `polyspec/hyper-native`. When the host packages take their native modules (S-4), installing them changes: PHP needs the extension `polyspec/hyper-native`, built by PIE for each PHP minor, which `polyspec/hyper` requires as `ext-polyspec_hyper`; the Python wheel becomes a wheel per platform with abi3 in place of `py3-none-any`; and the Node addon follows the packages per platform of plan T-2. When `hyper-cli` calls the rules of the core through the Node binding (H-8), it runs only on the platforms of T-2 and loses Windows, Intel macOS and musl, where the build in JavaScript runs now.
- The specification, the development spec and the recipe check name the moved host code (C-7): three statements of `contracts/protocol.json` name `polyspec.hyper.server` and its module `polyspec.hyper.server.server`, `contracts/interfaces.json` and the operation documents name the paths `packages/server/python/src/polyspec/hyper/server/` and the namespace `Polyspec\Hyper\Server`, the recipe readers and the script tests read them, and the fixture tree of the recipe check moves its Python package to `src/polyspec/hyper/server/` and its PHP classes to the new namespace. No reference to the old namespace or module path is left outside the changelog, the plans of finished tasks and the `from` values of the changes of the interface contract.
- The Python host code of the server is the package `polyspec.hyper.server`, where it was `polyspec.hyper` (binding P-1): the 65 files of `packages/server/python/src/polyspec/hyper/` move to `src/polyspec/hyper/server/`, `polyspec.hyper` is a namespace without an `__init__.py`, and the module of `create_server` is `polyspec.hyper.server.server`; the tests, the README, the board example, the package data test and the owner rules name the new paths. An application imports `polyspec.hyper.server` and its modules; the names of the classes and functions stay (C-7).
- The PHP host code of the server is the namespace `Polyspec\Hyper\Server`, where it was `Polyspec\Hyper` (binding P-1: a package of a repository with components adds its component to the namespace): the 55 source files and the tests of `packages/server/php`, its `composer.json` and the root `composer.json` with their locks, the board example, whose namespace is `Polyspec\Hyper\Server\Examples\Board`, the benchmark server and the namespaces of the generated programs in the Makefile. An application names `Polyspec\Hyper\Server\App` and the other classes of the public API by the new namespace; the class names and the error classes `Hyper<Category>Error` stay (C-7).
- The manifests of the Python and Node host packages take what their native modules need (binding L-2a): `packages/server/python/pyproject.toml` builds with maturin, the native module `polyspec.hyper.server._native` from `native/Cargo.toml` and the host code of `src/`, in place of setuptools, and `packages/server/ts/package.json` packs `native/generated/*.node`; `docs/operations/dependencies.md` states the range of maturin. The Python package does not build until the binding generator writes the source of its native module and the host code moves to `polyspec.hyper.server`. The contract check reports CK-19 only for the manifest of the PHP extension package, which the scaffold writes when it supports `packages/server/php/native/` (C-7).
- The Rust core and its interface contract take the names of a repository with components (binding P-1): the crate is `polyspec-hyper-server` with the library `polyspec_hyper_server`, the contract names the library hyper-server, Composer `polyspec/hyper-server` with the PHP namespace `Polyspec\Hyper\Server`, PyPI `polyspec-hyper-server` with the Python module `polyspec.hyper.server` and npm `@polyspec/hyper-server`, and the binding crates are `polyspec-hyper-server-python`, `polyspec-hyper-server-node` and `polyspec-hyper-server-php-ext` with the PHP extension `polyspec_hyper_server`. The error classes keep the names `Hyper<Category>Error`. The contract records the move of the host code from `Polyspec\Hyper` and `polyspec.hyper` as a change; the host code of the three servers has not moved yet (C-7).
- The Rust core and its binding crates are one Cargo workspace at the root of the repository (binding L-2, L-2a): the root `Cargo.toml` names `packages/server/rust` and the native modules `packages/server/python/native`, `packages/server/ts/native` and `packages/server/php-ext/native`, which the binding scaffold wrote, each with its `Cargo.toml` and `src/lib.rs` and the Node one with `build.rs`; `Cargo.lock`, `clippy.toml` and `rust-toolchain.toml` move to the root, the lock pins `polyspec-binding-core` at the end of its branch, once, and `.gitignore` leaves out `native/generated/`. The interface contract marks the three language packages as host packages (`host: true`). The contract check no longer reports CK-20 or a missing binding crate, and reports CK-19 for six entries of the host manifests, which the host packages take with the bindings (S-4).
- The Rust core takes `polyspec-binding-core` of the repository binding as its one kind interface, which every library of its lock uses, and the libraries `polyspec-session-store` and `polyspec-php-serialization` under their new names, with `polyspec-http-cookie`, `polyspec-json`, `polyspec-http-negotiation`, `polyspec-http-media-type` and `polyspec-template` at the end of their branches; the exception of `AGENTS.md` for the old names is gone, and its interface contract names the library session-store and the paths of `binding_core`. The release assets of php-serialization 0.0.6, which the Node and Python servers install, are named by the URL of the repository php-serialization and keep their package names `@polyspec/php-serialize` and `polyspec-php-serialize` until a release under the new names; `docs/operations/dependencies.md` states both (C-7, 9c).
- The opening of the session takes the flash data only for a page request (HY-120.6, which replaces HY-120.5), and a request to `<base path>/_hyper/keep` reads only the token and leaves the flash data in the session, because the kept endpoint answers no page and the flash data belongs to the next page request (HY-120.7); the case `a_keep_request_leaves_the_flash_values_to_the_next_page` of `contracts/app.json` states it, and the tests of the Rust core check it.
- The Rust core opens the session of a routed request through the store interface (C-7): `opening::open_page` takes the flash values and the changed topics, then reads the token, which it sets when the session holds none (HY-120.5, HY-25.2, HY-24.1), refuses a `POST` whose form token does not match with 403 (HY-24.4, HY-59.8), masks the token with the mask that the host draws (HY-24.2), and gives the input of the page with the changed topics and the kept server values of every region that keeps one (HY-19, HY-11.3, HY-40); `opening::keep_request` answers the kept endpoint with 403, 400 or 204 after it stores the value (HY-40.1), and takes no flash data, so that the next page still finds it. The interface contract lists the records `opening` and `keep_request`, the variant `opened` and these functions, which take the store unit of the session-store library.
- The Rust core takes `polyspec-http-negotiation` from the branch of its Git repository, which names `polyspec-http-media-type` by Git, and reads no other working tree; the specification, the features and the comments of the core name the libraries http-negotiation, http-form, php-serialization and session-store. The core keeps `polyspec-sessions` and `polyspec-php-serialize`, and every library on the commits whose kind interface is `polyspec-bindgen-core`, until the session-store library takes the kind interface of the other libraries (C-7, 9c).
- The Rust core routes a request before its page (C-7): `front::route_request` takes the pages, the `chosen` of the selection of client rendering, which the host calls (HY-62.9), and the method, the path, `Accept`, `HX-Request`, `Hyper-Context` and the media type of the body, and gives a plain refusal, the shell, the kept endpoint, a representation or a page, in the order of HY-59.3 and HY-120: the base path or the data base path (HY-8.2, HY-62.16 to HY-62.19), the answers outside the data base path (HY-62.22, HY-62.23, HY-62.34), `/_hyper/keep` with its 405 and its form type (HY-40, HY-59.12), the route (404), the media type with the page among the candidates also under the data base path, whose 406 lists no page (HY-116.13, HY-116.14), the method (HY-27.6), the 406 of a page under the data base path (HY-62.20), `Hyper-Context` (HY-127.2) and the form type of an action (HY-59.12). `front::page_headers` gives `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control`, `Vary` and the weak tag of a page with its 304 (HY-15.3, HY-52, HY-53, HY-65, HY-117.1), `front::frame_headers` the frame policy and the `no-store` of a failure (HY-45, HY-65.1), and `front::kept_value` the value that the kept endpoint stores (HY-40.1). The pages hold the base path, the data base path and the form types in the record `pages_options` of `Pages::open`, and the interface contract lists the records `front`, `plain`, `page_response`, `page_headers`, the variant `route` and these functions.
- `AGENTS.md` states the dependencies of the Rust core: the branch `v1/rust-core` of each polyspec repository until its release, `polyspec-http-negotiation` as a path dependency on the checkout beside this repository until that repository commits the name, `polyspec-php-serialize` until the sessions library takes the new name, and the binding crates in `packages/server/<lang>/native/` beside the host packages, whose absence the contract check of bindgen reports as CK-19 and CK-20 until it scaffolds that place (C-7).
- The pages of an application are an owned handle, `steps::Pages`, which `Pages::open` makes from the bytes of the manifest, the templates by name, the read paths of the build, the build id, the time zone and the declaration of a context handler, and whose `register` takes a function of the application before the first page request (HYC-24); a `PageRequest` holds the handle, so that a binding keeps it while the host calls the handlers. The interface contract lists the units `pages`, `page_request`, the record `page_input`, the enum `page_kind` and the variants `call`, `action_outcome`, `page_step_input`, `answer` and `step` (bindgen T-9), with the owner of `template_value`, and the contract check of bindgen passes them; its remaining findings ask for the binding packages beside the core (L-2), which S-4 places (C-7).
- Every failure of the open of an application has one error, `InvalidApplication`, with the kind `invalid_application` and a message that names its cause, also the path of the static shell, the data base path or the route under it (HY-62.37); the message of a representation entry names HY-118 and that of client rendering HY-62, and no other message gains `(HY-118)` (C-7).
- The Rust core takes the renamed libraries: `polyspec-json` of the repository json in place of `polyspec-ordered-json`, `polyspec-http-cookie` of http-cookie in place of `polyspec-http-cookies`, `polyspec-http-media-type` from its Git branch and the template language at the end of its branch, whose contract passes the checks of bindgen, so that the finding CK-10 of the interface contract is gone; the specification and the interface contract name the libraries json and http-cookie (C-7, 9c). `polyspec-http-negotiation` stays a path dependency until its branch holds the name, and `polyspec-php-serialize` stays until the sessions library, whose store interface uses its values, takes `polyspec-php-serialization`.
- The Rust core gives the page of a request as steps (C-7): `steps::PageRequest` is a state machine without I/O whose `next` asks the host to call one handler of the application, the action, the context handler, the shared handler, the loader of the route, of a route region or of a manifest region, and the reply, or gives the answer, a document, a JSON text or no page; the core holds no object of the language, a Node handler is awaited on the event loop, and the exception of a handler does not pass a frame of Rust, while a function of a template stays a callback of the engine, because a render is one call that the engine does not suspend. The steps call the context handler after an action that returns the page or lets `REVISION_CHANGED` escape and before the shared handler, and not after a redirect, a stop or a failure (HY-124.2 to HY-124.4), merge the data of the action into the route data (HY-26.2, HY-58.3), leave out an absent route region (HY-75.1) and give a page with status 200 the status of its reply (HY-69). The core also checks a declaration of client rendering, the path of the shell, its text and its declaration of the data base path, and the routes under it (HY-62.5, HY-62.6, HY-62.15, HY-62.39), and answers a chosen request outside the data base path with 404, 405, 400, 406 or the shell (HY-62.22, HY-62.23, HY-62.34). An open of client rendering that fails gives the kind `invalid_application` (HY-62.39, which replaces HY-62.35), and the cases of the group `openRejections` of `contracts/client.json` give it as `raises`; the Node case of the prefix `hyper: ` is gone (HY-106.19). The interface contract lists `check_shell_path` and `shell_text`.
- The Rust core renders a document with the template engine of the template language (C-7): `render::Templates` holds the templates of the application, which the host reads when the application opens, with the reserved template `hyper/data.tpl` (HY-31.3), and the functions of the application (HYC-24), which the engine calls while it renders and whose exception passes through unchanged; `render_document` renders the layout with the title, the embedded data, the build element and each manifest region rendered alone, and the page region with its present route regions (HY-12, HY-13), binding each root once (HY-13.5); a region that does not render with its kept values renders with its loader data and loses them, also in the embedded data, and a document that does not render then fails with the kind of the template language (HY-38.16 to HY-38.18). A failure of a render is the error `Template` with the kind and the category of the template language, and the interface contract forwards every kind of the template language. Its tests run the 3 cases of the group `parts` of `app.json` and the documents of the kept cases of `app.json` against the application `a_board`.
- The Rust core assembles a page (C-7): `page::shared_data` gives `title`, `csrf` and the values of the shared handler and fails with `shared_holds_context` for a returned `context` (HY-10.2, HY-124.8); `page::current_path` reads the current path of `HX-Current-URL` (HY-11.4); `page::changed_topics` gives the recorded topics and `path` (HY-19.1, HY-11.3); `page::regions` gives the regions of a document request and of a region request (HY-18); `page::kept_values` gives the `server` and `cookie` kept values that conform, by region and path (HY-38.8); `page::json_value` gives the JSON value of a response in the order of HY-17 with `build` and `context` (HY-128.1); and `page::embeds` and `page::embedded_data` give whether a document embeds its data and that data (HY-31.2, HY-31.3). Its tests run the 8 cases of the current path in `request.json` and rule tests of the rest, and the interface contract lists `current_path`. The rendering of the templates stays in the three servers.
- The Rust core decides the kept values (HY-38) and the context (HY-124 to HY-128): `keep::select` gives the kept values that conform to the region data by path, each applied before the next, and `keep::apply` the data with them (HY-38.2 to HY-38.9, HY-38.13, HY-38.19); `keep::cookie_values` reads the cookie `hy-keep`, where a value outside the data model drops only that value (HY-38.2, HY-38.11); `context::with_context` places the context in the shared data or fails with `shared_holds_context` (HY-10.3, HY-124.8); `context::text`, `context::hash` and `context::member` give the JSON text, the hash and the member `context` of a response (HY-125.10, HY-125.12, HY-128.2); and `context::valid_header` checks `Hyper-Context` (HY-127.2). Its tests run the 34 cases of `keep.json` and the 11 cases of the groups `contexts` and `withContext` of `context.json`, and the interface contract lists `context_hash` and `valid_context_header` (C-7).
- The steps of the Node and Python servers read the store cases of file sessions that the sessions library holds, 17 requests and the options that the store refuses, from `tests/legacy/file-store-sessions.json`, a fixed copy under another name, because `contracts/file-sessions.json` keeps the cases of hyper; the steps of that copy run the requests as those of `contracts/file-sessions.json` do, and S-4 removes it with `tests/legacy` (C-6). The recipe check finds nothing.
- The lock of the Rust core takes the current commits of the branch `v1/rust-core` of bindgen, http-cookies, php-serialize, sessions and template, with one copy of `polyspec-bindgen-core`; `polyspec-http-negotiate` and `polyspec-http-media-type` stay path dependencies, because the branch of http-negotiate still names http-media-type by a path (C-7).
- The recipe check finds again only what the servers lack: the concern `app` names HY-132 and the concern `build` names HY-106, whose failures the cases of `app.json` and `build.json` state; the case of `request.json` with a negative limit no longer requires HY-114, which its field `raises` states; and the steps of the Node and Python servers that read the Redis store cases read `tests/legacy/redis-sessions.json`, a fixed copy of the case file that hyper held before the sessions library took its cases, which S-4 removes with `tests/legacy` (C-6).
- The steps of the three servers that read the form cases read `tests/legacy/fields.json`, a fixed copy of `contracts/fields.json` as hyper held it before http-forms took these cases, with its declaration in `tests/legacy/interfaces.json`, whose sentences name the statements that point to http-forms; the case readers of PHP, Node and Python and the recipe check take the case files of `tests/legacy` with those of `contracts/`, and S-4 removes the directory when the servers call the core (C-3). The field `selected` of the section `select` of `accept.json` names its statement HY-116.19, where the declaration held a case in its place, so that the declaration is valid again.
- The rules of reading a form belong to http-forms (C-3): the query values, the form values, the files, the header lines of a part, the reader of a body in chunks, its first failure, the directory of a request, `moveTo` and `fileOver` are the rules F-6, F-7, F-8 to F-12, F-16 and F-20 to F-27 of http-forms, and the statements of hyper that stated them are retired for statements that name those rules (HY-56.9, HY-57.13, HY-113.21, HY-113.22, HY-114.11, HY-114.12, HY-131.26 to HY-131.29). What changes: a parameter that the type of a body does not define changes nothing (F-12.4), where a parameter of an `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body other than `charset` failed with kind `unknown_parameter` and status 400 (HY-57.14); a failed move gives the kinds `exists` and `moved` of http-forms (F-11). hyper keeps the API of a request, the limits that an application declares with their defaults, the status 413 and its text, the removal of the directory after the hooks, the order of the checks and the reading of the Node host. `contracts/fields.json` is removed, because http-forms holds its cases; the steps of the three servers that ran them stay until the servers call the core (S-4).
- The cases of `contracts/app.json` give an application that fails to open as `raises` with the kind `invalid_application`, or `invalid_build` for its build file, the category `value` and the message only where HY-106.11 states it; the key `openError` of a case is gone (S-3b, HY-106.21).
- A number of the data model that is an integer within ±(2^53 − 1) is written to a session record as a PHP integer, read from the bits of the number without a rounding conversion, and every other number as a float (HY-104.34); a stored kept region of another form fails every read and write of the kept values, also of another region (HY-104.35). The Rust core takes `polyspec-bindgen-core`, `polyspec-ordered-json`, `polyspec-template`, `polyspec-http-cookies`, `polyspec-sessions` and `polyspec-php-serialize` from the branch `v1/rust-core` of their Git repositories, so that it reads the committed state of each library and not a working tree; `polyspec-http-negotiate` and `polyspec-http-media-type` stay path dependencies until the branch of http-negotiate names http-media-type by Git (C-7).
- The Rust core keeps the values of hyper in a session through the store interface of the sessions library (C-7, HY-104.25): the token, a new one of 32 random bytes and a new one at a renewal (HY-24.1, HY-24.14, HY-72.2), the flash values and the changed topics, which the next request takes (HY-25.2), and the kept server values by region and path (HY-40), converted between the record, a PHP value of php-serialize, and the data model (HY-104.13, HY-104.14), where a stored value of another form fails with `invalid_session_record`, whose category is `store` in the cases of `contracts/session.json` as in HY-132 (HY-104.23); the tests run the 30 cases of the groups `token`, `flash` and `kept`. The core depends on `polyspec-sessions` without its stores and on `polyspec-php-serialize`, and the lock holds one copy of `polyspec-bindgen-core`. The interface contract passes on the kinds of the template data model and of the sessions library, and the error class `RecordError` forwards the offset of a record that cannot be decoded.
- An integer of a reply outside ±(2^53 − 1), a `Max-Age` or a page status that PHP and Python can give, fails with the kind `integer_range` of hyper before its sign or its value is checked (HY-52.18, HY-69.9), and Node refuses such a number with `invalid_number` of its binding, because a Node number outside the safe integers is no integer; `integer_range` joins the table of HY-132, and the three cases of `contracts/reply.json` for PHP and Python expect it (S-3b, C-7).
- The Rust core keeps of the data only its read paths (HY-73.29, HY-73.22) by the tree of read paths of the build, and its tests run the 24 cases of `contracts/reads.json` (C-7).
- The cases of the session stores belong to the sessions library, which holds them now: `contracts/redis-sessions.json` is removed, and `contracts/file-sessions.json` keeps the two cases of the record conversion of hyper (HY-104.13, HY-104.14), the case of a record that holds no array and its group `cookie` (HY-45). The specification follows the sessions library (C-6): an identifier has 22 to 256 characters (HY-104.31; SE-5.1), the options of the stores fail with the kinds `invalid_directory`, `invalid_lifetime` and `invalid_lock_expire` (HY-104.32, HY-115.28), `lockExpire` has no default and `lockWait` counts microseconds (HY-115.28, HY-115.29), and no server removes expired sessions while it answers a request: the application calls `collect` of its store (HY-104.33; SF-5.3). The core depends on `polyspec-sessions-core`, whose store interface the host functions take, and the interface contract records that `ArraySession` is the `MemoryStore` of the sessions library.
- The Rust core reads and checks the manifest (HY-1, HY-2, HY-37, HY-40.2, HY-118, HY-4.6), routes a request path with rest parameters and a base path (HY-4 to HY-8, HY-49), and reads and writes the JSON text of hyper with ordered-json and the template data model (HY-54.18 to HY-54.21); its tests run `manifest.json`, `routes.json`, `rest.json` and `json.json` (C-7). The manifest file is read as HY-54.20 reads JSON text (HY-2.9): a key that starts with U+0000 is read, and a manifest deeper than the 64 levels of the data model fails the open, where 511 nested containers were read; `contracts/manifest.json` gives its failures as `raises`. The kinds of the data model are the snake_case kinds of the template data model, such as `data_number_range`.
- hyper makes no range check of its own on a `Max-Age` or a page status of a reply: the binding refuses a value that it does not take as an integer with `invalid_number` (HY-52.17, HY-69.8), so Node refuses 2^53 while PHP and Python write it or refuse it as another integer, and the three cases of `contracts/reply.json` are split by runtime; the kind `invalid_number` leaves the table of HY-132. The 406 listing states that its combinations follow the declared order of the served values (HY-116.13), the JSON rules name the requirements of ordered-json (RQ-004 to RQ-035) in HY-54.18 to HY-54.21, and the interface contract declares `page_status` and `reply_status` with the return type `uint` (S-3b, C-7).
- The Rust core takes `polyspec-bindgen-core` from the Git repository of bindgen, as every core of polyspec does, so that one copy of the kind interface serves the core and its dependencies (C-7).
- The interface contract of hyper is `packages/server/rust/interface.json` in the schema of bindgen (C-7, design I-1): the packages `polyspec/hyper`, `polyspec-hyper` and `@polyspec/hyper`, the error classes `HyperValueError`, `HyperTypeError`, `HyperIoError`, `HyperStoreError`, `HyperBusyError` and `HyperInternalError`, the kinds of HY-132 and those of http-negotiate and http-cookies that hyper passes on, the internal functions of the core that the hosts will call, the host functions `listener`, `server`, `run`, `create_server`, `create_wsgi` and `create_asgi` (HY-106.20), and the changes from the current API of S-3b. `docs/features.md` lists the rules that stay in the three servers until the core holds them and the servers call it (S-4).
- The Rust core of the server rules is the crate `polyspec-hyper` at `packages/server/rust` (C-7), with `make core-test`, `make core-lint` and `make core-fmt-check`: the errors of HY-132 with their kinds, statuses and categories; the selection of the media type of a request with the weights of http-negotiate, its order among equal weights, the 406 listing and the `Vary` of each response (HY-116, HY-117, HY-119); the manifest check of a representation entry, its methods and `Allow`, the checks of its body and of the response of its handler (HY-118, HY-121, HY-122); the weak and the strong tags (HY-53, HY-122.10); the names of the cookies of hyper and the session cookie written by http-cookies (HY-45, HY-38.10); the cookies, the `Cache-Control`, the page status and the `Referrer-Policy` of a reply (HY-52, HY-69, HY-103); application paths and page statuses (HY-46, HY-58); the token, its mask, its check and `browser()` (HY-24); the answers of the errors of the ORM by kind (HY-107); and the order of the steps of a request and which request opens a session (HY-120). Its tests run the cases of `csrf.json`, `result.json`, `reply.json`, `accept.json` and `media-type.json` that the core decides. The kind `invalid_representation` joins the table of HY-132, and the case of `result.json` with a location that is not UTF-8 text expects `invalid_text` of the binding. The three servers do not use the crate yet (S-4).
- The contract cases follow the binding rules of numbers and the session stores of the three servers (S-3b): a float `Max-Age` or page status such as `60.0` is no integer in PHP and Python and fails with `invalid_number`, while Node, whose number has no float type, takes it (HY-52.14), so each of the four such cases of `contracts/reply.json` is split into a Node case and a PHP and Python case; the cases of `contracts/file-sessions.json` and `contracts/redis-sessions.json` that only Node and Python ran apply to the three servers (HY-104.26, HY-115.25), and the Node case that compared a message with the prefix `hyper: ` compares no message (HY-106.19).
- Every failure of hyper is an error with a kind, a status and a category (S-3b; HY-132): the error is of the class `HyperValueError`, `HyperTypeError`, `HyperIoError`, `HyperStoreError`, `HyperBusyError` or `HyperInternalError` of its category, which extends the base class of that category, gives `kind` and `status`, and a caller tells failures apart by the kind; the table of HY-132 lists the kinds of hyper, and an error of a library that hyper uses keeps the kind of that library. What changes: the open error is kind `invalid_application` and the build file kind `invalid_build`, where PHP, Node and Python raised exactly `InvalidArgumentException`, `Error` and `ValueError` and the subclass `InvalidBuild` (HY-106.21); no message has the Node prefix `hyper: ` (HY-106.19); an argument of another type is refused by the binding with `invalid_argument_type` or `invalid_number` and no message of hyper, where the servers raised `TypeError` with the messages of HY-52, HY-25, HY-60, HY-69 and HY-103, and a float `Max-Age` such as `60.0` is no integer in PHP and Python (HY-52.14, HY-52.15, HY-25.7, HY-60.32, HY-69.7, HY-103.9); a value outside the data model fails with the kind and the message of the template data model (HY-44.9); a character above U+00FF in a request fails with `invalid_text` (HY-42.7); a request without a selection fails with `not_selected` (HY-116.21); an error of the ORM is recognized by the error classes of the orm package and the kinds `no_rows` and `revision_changed`, so hyper depends on the orm package (HY-107.7 to HY-107.9); and the host entries are functions of the host code that take the application first, `listener(app, ...)`, `server(app, ...)` and `run($app, ...)`, and no members of `App` (HY-106.20). The contract cases give each failure as `raises` with `kind`, `status`, `category` and `message`, and the keys `error`, `class`, `fails` and `failure` of a failure are gone from `result.json`, `reply.json`, `csrf.json`, `build.json`, `session.json`, `media-type.json`, `context.json`, `fields.json`, `file-sessions.json` and `redis-sessions.json`; a thrown ORM error of a case is `{"class", "kind"}`. The three servers implement the retired rules until they use the core (S-4).
- The specification draws the line between the session values of hyper and the storage of the sessions library (S-3b; HY-104.25): the token, the flash values and the changed topics, the kept values, the conversion of a record to the data model and the keys `_hyper_*` are rules of hyper, and the identifier, the record bytes, the lock, the file or Redis key and the expiry are rules of the sessions library, whose store interface hyper uses. What changes: a session that holds another value in the place of the token fails the request (HY-24.14), where it got a new token; the PHP server keeps its sessions in the file store of the sessions library and not in the native session handler, and `sessionSetup` of `App::open` is gone (HY-104.26); every server waits for the lock of a session file with a blocking `flock` on a thread of its own, without the retries of 50 ms of the Node server (HY-104.27, HY-104.29); a store that an application writes implements the store interface, and its exceptions reach the caller as they were raised (HY-104.30); the Redis store takes connection settings instead of a client function, asks Sentinel for the primary before each request with a session in every server, and every server answers 500 for a request that lost its lock (HY-115.23 to HY-115.27); and the PHP server gets the session identifier from the parse of HY-129 (HY-129.16). HY-24.9, HY-24.11, HY-104.4 to HY-104.6, HY-104.16 to HY-104.18, HY-104.24, HY-115.6, HY-115.9, HY-115.10, HY-115.14, HY-115.15, HY-115.19 and HY-129.10 are retired, and the case `a_stored_token_of_another_form_fails_the_request` of `contracts/app.json` replaces the case of a new token. The three servers implement the retired rules until they use the core (S-4).
- The specification writes JSON text with the serializer of ordered-json and reads it with `parse` of ordered-json and VAL-12 of the template data model (S-3b; HY-54.18 to HY-54.21, HY-121.11): a number is written as VAL-9 writes it, so `-0` is `0`, `1e-5` is `0.00001` and `5e-324` is `5e-324`; U+2028 and U+2029 are written as themselves; the reading limit is 256 nested containers and the data model allows 64 levels, with two kinds; a key that starts with U+0000 is a key; a number outside ±(2^53 − 1) fails with the error of the data model whatever its literal; and a text that ordered-json refuses fails with kind `malformed_json`. Before, every server wrote the bytes of PHP `json_encode`, read 511 levels and refused a key that starts with U+0000. HY-54.4 to HY-54.8 and HY-121.8 are retired. `contracts/json.json` gives its failures as `raises` and gains four cases of depth, and the case of `contracts/app.json` that refused a manifest key that starts with U+0000 is removed. The three servers implement the retired rules until they use the core (S-4).
- The specification reads the cookies of a request with `parse` of http-cookies and writes every cookie with `set_cookie` (S-3b): a host passes the `Cookie` lines that it received without joining them, and a runtime that gives them joined passes one line (HY-129.13 to HY-129.15; CK-2.4); the session cookie of every server and the cookies of a reply are written in one form, `<name>=<value>`, `; Max-Age=<seconds>` when given, `; Path=/`, `; Secure` under the conditions of HY-45, `; HttpOnly` and `; SameSite=Lax` (HY-45.8, HY-52.13; CK-9), where the Node and Python servers wrote the session cookie in the lower case form of PHP (`path=/; secure`) and a reply wrote `Max-Age` last. HY-129.1 to HY-129.5, HY-129.7 to HY-129.9, HY-129.12, HY-45.7, HY-52.3 and HY-54.16 are retired. The `Set-Cookie` values of `contracts/reply.json`, `contracts/app.json` and `contracts/file-sessions.json` follow. The three servers implement the retired rules until they use the core (S-4).
- The specification reads `Accept` with `Accept.parse` of http-negotiate and weighs the types of a route with `Accept.weigh` (HY-116.17 to HY-116.20, HY-119.5), and writes the `Content-Type` of a representation with `format` of http-media-type (HY-119.4) (S-3b). What changes: each `Accept` field line is read on its own, so a quoted string does not continue into the next line (NG-5.2); anything after the weight, an accept extension included, is a 400 (NG-5.5; RFC 9110, appendix B.3), where it was ignored; a trailing `;` is an empty parameter and no longer a 400 (MT-3.2); a parameter given twice is a 400 (NG-3.5); and the values of `charset` compare without case (NG-8.4). HY-116.3 to HY-116.10, HY-119.1, HY-119.2 and HY-119.3 are retired. `contracts/accept.json` gives its failures as `raises` with the kinds `invalid_accept` and `invalid_weight` and changes four cases, and the case `a_parameter_after_q_is_a_bad_request` of `contracts/representations.json` replaces the case of an accept extension. The three servers implement the retired rules until they use the core (S-4).
- The specification reads a `Content-Type` by MT-4 of http-media-type, as F-12 of http-forms states, and the contract cases follow (S-3b): a `Content-Type` that is no media type, the empty value included, is a 400 with kind `malformed_content_type` where HY-59.7 answered 415 and HY-57.10 and HY-113.11 read a body of another type (HY-59.12, HY-59.13, HY-57.11); SP and HTAB before the media type are ignored also for an urlencoded body, and an urlencoded type with a parameter other than `charset` fails with `unknown_parameter`; a `multipart/form-data` type without a boundary or with an empty one fails with `no_boundary` (HY-113.17); a quoted value without its end fails the body (HY-113.20); a part without `Content-Disposition`, with two of them, of another disposition type, without a name or with a parameter given twice fails the body (HY-113.19); `fileOver(limit)` fails with `invalid_number` for a limit that is no unsigned integer (HY-114.10); the first failure of a body is the one whose byte comes first (HY-131.23), and a `Content-Length` that is no number is a 400 (HY-131.24); the Node host feeds what `request.read()` gives in paused mode (HY-131.25). HY-59.7, HY-59.11, HY-57.4, HY-57.10, HY-113.6, HY-113.11, HY-113.13, HY-113.14, HY-114.9 and HY-131.21 are retired. In `contracts/fields.json` 15 cases are replaced by failing cases with `raises`, `contracts/request.json` gains four failing cases and `contracts/app.json` answers an empty `Content-Type` of the keep endpoint with 400. The three servers implement the retired rules until they use the core (S-4).
- The URLs of the php-serialize release assets in `package.json`, the npm locks, `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock` name the repository `polyspec/php-serialization`, the new name of `polyspec/php-serialize`; the assets and their integrity are the same (H28.20).

## 0.0.40

- `config/rebuild.json` classifies the files that H28.13, H28.15 and H28.16 added: the two module scripts of the board example and the parts and the scope readers of the recipe check, so `make rebuild-check` passes; it failed on these nine files in the CI run of the version commit of 0.0.40.
- `config/release.json` lists `scripts/recipe/typescript/package.json`, the private install of the TypeScript compiler API that the Node reader of the recipe check reads, as not released, so `make release-coverage` passes; it failed on that unclassified manifest (H28.16).

- HY-106.4 states that the PHP `query()`, `form()` and `files()` of a request return a list of pairs of a name and its values, as the code and HY-56 and HY-113 do; before, it said that they exposed `IteratorAggregate` (H28.18).

- `AGENTS.md` states that a task withdrawn with `[-]` is done: a task whose cause names a retry condition resumes once it holds, and one whose cause names none, or whose condition does not hold, stays done.

- `composer.json` and `examples/board/composer.json` require the json-schema fork by the range `^2.6.0-p2` instead of the exact constraint `2.6.0-p2`, and both locks still pin the release `v2.6.0-p2` with its dist, so the installed fork does not change. Before, `composer validate --strict` of both roots failed (exit 1) on the warning that Composer 2.10.3 gives for an exact constraint; now it passes (H28.17).

- `config/layout.yml` declares the PHP and Python readers of the recipe check that read the scopes and the functions of a server (`read-php-functions.php`, `read-php-scopes.php`, `PhpScope.php`, `read_python_scopes.py`) as exceptions of S9, with the reason of the two earlier readers: they read the code with the reflection and the tokens of PHP and the ast module of Python, which TypeScript does not have (H28.16).

- The units of the three servers lie in one directory for each group of concerns, the same nine in PHP, Node and
  Python: `app`, `host`, `http`, `result`, `form`, `session`, `data`, `page` and `representation` (HYR-4), and
  `contracts/interfaces.json` declares the groups and the group of each concern, which `make recipe-check` checks.
  An application takes every public name from the entry of the package (HY-106).
  What changes for an application:
  - Python: a public name is imported from the package `polyspec.hyper`, and the module paths of the earlier
    releases no longer exist. `from polyspec.hyper.app import App`, `from polyspec.hyper.request import Request`,
    `from polyspec.hyper.file_sessions import FileSessions`, `from polyspec.hyper.server import create_server`,
    `from polyspec.hyper.wsgi import create_wsgi` and `from polyspec.hyper.asgi import create_asgi` become
    `from polyspec.hyper import App, Request, FileSessions, create_server, create_wsgi, create_asgi`. The same holds
    for `ArraySession`, `BadRequest`, `Choice`, `ClientRendering`, `Container`, `Forbidden`, `MediaType`,
    `NotFound`, `Redirect`, `RedisSessions`, `Reply`, `Representation`, `Response`, `Result`, `SessionLockError`,
    `SessionStore` and `UploadedFile`.
  - PHP: no class name changes; every class stays in the namespace `Polyspec\Hyper`. `composer install` or
    `composer dump-autoload` writes the autoload map of the new directories.
  - Node: nothing changes; `@polyspec/hyper-server` exports the same names from the same entry. (H28.15)

- `make recipe-check` proves for every member that two or more servers have that it does the same job with the same result (H28.16, HYR-5, HYR-7, HYR-8): each section of `caseFiles` names the members whose result it states, the check reads the step of each language and the class of each value it calls from what the step writes, and it fails a public member that no section names, a section whose step does not call a member it names, an internal member that no stated member reaches, and a member that one server reaches from other members than another does. On this tree it reports 149 members stated directly and 125 internal and 114 private members stated through their callers. To reach that, the servers changed where they differed: the internal `Accept.select`, which no server called, is removed from the three servers, and `accept.json#select` states the weighting through `parse` and `best`; `close` is a member of the record session of the three servers; the Python `ArraySession` and record session extend `SessionStore`; and the Python `FileSessions` tries a held lock again after a pause, as the Node store does, instead of waiting in `flock` (HY-104.6).

- The PHP server answers a request whose session store fails to open with the plain 500 before the application answers, and only the write hook receives it, as the Node and Python servers do (HY-60.14, HY-60.16, HY-60.17): `NativeSession::start` raises the internal `SessionOpenError` with the message of the failure, which the answer does not handle. Before, the PHP server turned the failure into a handled 500 that reached the response hook. The scenario `session-open-error-is-the-plain-500` of `tests/scripts/upload-servers.test.mts` states it for the three servers (H28.16).

- The three servers give the same result for the public members that only native tests stated, and shared cases state them (H28.16): the new `contracts/media-type.json` and `contracts/response.json`, and the sections `fields.json#uploaded`, `session.json#store`, `client.json#declaration` and `representations.json#values`, with the fields `mediaType`, `limits` and `over` of `request.json`, `held` and `location` of `result.json` and `template` of `manifest.json#reads`; HY-106.17, HY-104.24, HY-59.11 and HY-114.9 state the response, the session store, `mediaType()` and `fileOver(limit)`. A kept value and the body of `etag(body)` outside the data model now fail when they are given, with the error of the data model, in the PHP and Python servers as in the Node server (HY-40.4, HY-122.13); before, PHP and Python stored the kept value and tagged the body. The Node selection of a client rendering must return `new Choice(chosen, value)`, as in PHP and Python (HY-62.8); a plain object `{ chosen, value }` now fails the request with the 500 of HY-43. The PHP `App::through` answers through `respond` when no delivery is given, and the Node and Python hosts release the request with `release` after its hooks. The case readers of PHP and Python fail a case that lacks a field that is not `optional`, as the Node reader does. The Python tests that the cases replaced are removed (37 of `render_requests.py` and `render_files.py`, with the Makefile target `python-file-parts-test`), and the 44 tests of the WSGI and ASGI callables, which only the Python server has, are named under their members in `contracts/interfaces.json`. `make recipe-run-php` (1428 cases), `make recipe-run-node` (1443) and `make recipe-run-python` (1450) pass.

- The request has `browser()` in the three servers (HY-24.10 to HY-24.13, HY-106.12): PHP `Request::browser(): ?string`, Node `browser(): string | null` and Python `browser() -> Optional[str]` return the lower-case hexadecimal SHA-256 digest of the session token, which is the same in every request of one session until the token is renewed, and none for a request that opens no session; the token itself is not given. Before, an application had only `csrfToken()`, which is masked anew for every response, so no value named the browser of a request. The internal `Csrf.browserOf` computes the digest from a masked token. The nine cases of `contracts/csrf.json#browser`, three cases of `contracts/app.json` and one of `contracts/representations.json` pass in the three servers (H28.14).
- A call of a public member with more arguments than its parameters fails with `TypeError` in the three servers (HY-106.8, HY-106.14 to HY-106.16): PHP raises its `ArgumentCountError` for an extra argument through the internal `ArgumentCount::most` at the start of 89 public members, as PHP itself does for a missing one; Node checks the count of 75 public members and the members of its six options records with the internal `ArgumentCountError`, a `TypeError`. Before, PHP and Node ignored an extra argument and Node a missing one. The 268 cases of the new `contracts/arguments.json` pass in the three servers (H28.11-3).

- The browser code creates the module scripts of a head (HYC-25). When it shows the head of a client-rendered document, of the layout of a region response or of a history restoration document, it creates a `script` element at the end of the head of the page for each `script` of the type `module` with a `src` whose address the page does not hold, in the order of the head, sets its `async` to false unless the source script has the attribute `async`, so that the created scripts run in the order of the head, shows the content only after the created scripts have loaded, and removes no script afterwards. A script or a stylesheet link that fails to load removes the scripts and the links of that application and marks the region or the body with `hy-error="0"` (HYC-14). Before, the browser code read only the stylesheet links of a head, so a module script that the layout names on one page was not loaded after a navigation to that page. The static shell declares the URL of the entry that it inlines with `<meta name="hyper-entry">`, the member `hyper` of `manifest.json` (HYC-16), so a layout that names the entry does not load it again; in a shell without it, a head that names a module script that the page does not hold fails with `hy-error="0"`. The board example names the module scripts `reader.js` and `after-reader.js` on the post page, and `tests/e2e/board.spec.ts` checks in both rendering modes that a navigation runs each once in the order of the layout, also when the first arrives after the second, that the client entry runs once, and that a script that fails to load marks the region. What changes for an application: build the assets again, so that the shell declares its entry; a module script of the head of a layout now loads on navigation and runs before the new content is in the page; an object given as the option `document` names its member `head` instead of `stylesheets`; `StylesheetError` and the new `ScriptError` extend the new `HeadError`; `@polyspec/hyper-client` adds `HeadError`, `HeadPart`, `applyHead`, `PageScripts`, `ScriptError`, `scriptKey`, `ScriptElement`, `ScriptPage` and `PageStylesheets.insert`, and renames no other exported name. Verification: `make hyper-test-js` (171 tests), `make hyper-test-scripts TESTS=tests/scripts/build-assets.test.mts` and `make e2e` (20 flows) passed (H28.13).

- Every pin of the template release moves from v0.0.26 to v0.0.27: `TEMPLATE_VERSION` of the Makefile, the tarball URLs of `package.json` and `tests/package-install/package.json`, the exact requirements of `packages/client`, `packages/build`, `packages/server/ts` and `packages/server/php`, the Composer repository and requirement of `composer.json` and `examples/board/composer.json`, and the wheel URL with its sha256 in `packages/server/python/pyproject.toml`. npm, Composer and uv wrote `package-lock.json`, `tests/package-install/package-lock.json`, `composer.lock`, `examples/board/composer.lock` and `packages/server/python/uv.lock`, and `tests/scripts/template-version.test.mts` expects 0.0.27. In this release the include check of `@polyspec/template-compiler` accepts an `any?` value into an `any` input, so the build accepts an included template that reads a name of the root; the PHP library, the native extension, the Node package and the Python wheel differ from 0.0.26 only in their version (H28.12).
- `contracts/app.json` states the page application in shared cases: 236 cases of one request to a declarative application (handlers as lists of steps, options of `open`, stored session values, requests before the compared one) and 32 cases of `open`, run by the three servers. `contracts/hooks.json` states that a hook that is no function fails the creation in Node and Python, `contracts/context.json` gains nine cases, `contracts/result.json` three, `contracts/session.json` three, `contracts/client.json` two and `contracts/file-sessions.json` one. The servers change as the cases state: a member `values` or `changed` of the stored flash that is null, and stored kept values of a region that are null, fail in PHP (HY-104.23), and a limit of `App.open` that is no integer fails the open in Python (HY-59.2, HY-66.1, HY-114.6, HY-131.10). `App` of Node holds the checks of its path options and of the template index in the private members `absolutePaths`, `templateIndex` and `routeTemplates`, and `Renderer` of PHP the loading of the generated program in `program`. The case reader of Node reads an integer outside the safe range, gives a case with `only` to the runners that it names, and compares the values of `subset` as `exact` does. The native tests that the cases replace are removed: the PHP tests go from 120 to 46, the Node tests from 130 to 28 and the Python tests from 132 to 92. HY-18.3, HY-100.33 and HY-106.11 are new, and HY-104.16 names the tests that check the lock.

- Shared cases state behaviour that only native tests of each language stated, and the servers follow them where they differed. New case files: `contracts/session.json` (24 cases of the session unit in the three servers), `contracts/file-sessions.json` (27) and `contracts/redis-sessions.json` (14) for the Node and Python session stores, and `contracts/manifest.json` (36). New sections: `targets` and `values` of `contracts/request.json` (55 cases), `openRejections` of `contracts/client.json` (8) and `outputs` of `contracts/build.json` (7); `contracts/client.json` gains 23 cases, `contracts/representations.json` two and `contracts/reads.json` one. A section of `caseFiles` names the members whose result its cases state (`members`, HYR-9). The servers change as the cases state: a stored flash value or kept value of another form fails the request (HY-104.23) in the three; the region name `build` is refused in the three, and `layout`, `title` and `data` for a route region in Python (HY-2.3); the current path is the UTF-8 text of its bytes and absent for bytes that are not UTF-8 (HY-11.4); a lifetime or lock expiry that is no positive integer fails the creation of a Python session store (HY-104.19, HY-115.21); a choice whose `chosen` is no boolean answers 500 in Python (HY-62.36); a disconnect hook that is no function fails `App.open` of Node (HY-67.13); and the `http.server` host reads a body sent in chunks (HY-59.10). `tests/scripts/upload-servers.test.mts` runs 20 scenarios of the host layer against the three real servers: the response hook, the write hook, the request hook, a client that closes the connection, the session cookie, the body limit, public files, several cookie lines and the request method. `contracts/protocol.json` gains 32 statements. The native tests that the cases replace are removed: the PHP tests go from 153 to 120, the Node tests from 179 to 130 and the Python tests from 188 to 132.

- The application, the page and the host files have one shape in the three servers, and `contracts/interfaces.json` declares their members: 94 members, among them `App` with 25 members of the three servers under one name, number of parameters and visibility, and 17 Node types. The page rendering is the unit `Page` (`Page.php`, `page.ts`, `_page.py`) with `respond`, which `App` creates at open. Node holds its host in `http-host.ts`, the abort of a closed connection on the request (`RequestInit.signal`, `Request.stopClosed`) and `Disconnected` in a file of its own; `negotiate.ts`, `open-checks.ts` and `error-code.ts` are folded into their users. Python holds `Exchange`, `FileResponse` and `WsgiBody` in files of their own, and its package exports `App`, `create_server`, `create_wsgi`, `create_asgi`, `FileSessions`, `RedisSessions` and `SessionLockError`. The log of an unhandled error is stated for each server (HY-43.4), and `sessionSetup` of `App::open` (HY-104.18). The recipe check reads the options record only for a member whose one input is the record, and the member `iter` of a Python class is its `__iter__` (HYR-5). A PHP test states that the disconnect hook is called when the script ends during the answer of a closed connection.

- The three servers give the same answer where they differed. Under the data base path a method that the route does not allow receives 405 before the 406 of a request that selects `text/html`; the Node and Python servers answered 406 (HY-62.20, HY-116.12). A JSON response of a route whose document embeds its data omits `context.value` for a request with the current hash; the Node server always sent the value (HY-128). A loader that calls `renewSession()` while the 409 page of `REVISION_CHANGED` renders fails with the plain 500; the PHP and Python servers answered 409 (HY-72.4). The 409 page of an action that renewed the session carries a masked value of the new token; the Node server sent one of the old token (HY-72.3). A loader that raises `REVISION_CHANGED` for the page of an action fails with the plain 500; the three servers answered 409 (HY-107.4). A route region without a loader is present with no data of its own; the Python server left it absent (HY-75.7). The open fails for a shell path that is not absolute, a shell file that is missing or unreadable, and shell bytes that are not valid UTF-8, with the open error of each runtime (HY-62.35). The WSGI and ASGI hosts read HTTPS from `wsgi.url_scheme` and `scheme` (HY-45.6), every host passes the request method in upper case to the application, and the `http.server` host passes every method, where it answered 501 for a method outside seven (HY-100.31), and logs an escaped error as the other Python hosts do, and a representation handler of the Python server receives the bound services (HY-105.1). `contracts/context.json` holds the application `pages` with seven cases, and `contracts/client.json` one case.

- The session stores of the Node and Python servers have one design, each unit in a file of its own, and `contracts/interfaces.json` declares their 53 members and three option types. `RecordSession` takes the identifier of the cookie, and the store of a request extends it and gives its record with `stored()`; the Node class took two functions, and the two languages named its members apart. `FileSession`, `RedisSession`, `SessionLockError` and, in Node, `SessionTurns` have files of their own, the helpers are static methods of their classes, and the session cookie name and header are `RecordSession.cookieName` and `cookieHeader` in both. The Python server fails a session value outside the data model when a request stores it, as the Node server does; it wrote the value. HY-104.17 states the queue of one Node process, which a store leaves when it closes. Node no longer exports `FileSession`, `RecordSession` and `RedisSession`, which no application creates, and the Python modules are `_record_session`, `_file_session`, `_redis_session` and `session_lock_error`. `scripts/contract-programs.mts` builds each application in a staging directory and publishes it (HR-82): two test targets that ran at once removed the output of each other. `make recipe-check` reports 124 members that the spec does not hold, 52 fewer (H28.11).

- The session store interface, the store in memory, the session and the flash are one unit in one file in the three servers, and `contracts/interfaces.json` declares their 21 members. Node holds them in `session-store.ts`, `array-session.ts`, `session.ts` and `flash.ts`; its store in memory is `ArraySession`, as in PHP and Python, where it was `MemorySessionStore`, and `Flash` is a class. Python holds them in `session_store.py`, `array_session.py`, `_session.py` and `_flash.py`, and the module `polyspec.hyper.session` is removed. `ArraySession` has a constructor and the internal method `renewals()` in the three, and HY-106.1 names it as public API. The helper that reads a stored map is the private `storedMap` of `Session` in the three. `make recipe-check` reports 176 members that the spec does not hold, 51 fewer. The row H28.15 records the grouping of the units in a directory for each concern (H28.11).

- `contracts/interfaces.json` declares the 33 members of the units that only the PHP server has: `NativeSession`, `RedisClusterSessions`, `RedisSentinelSessions` and `Delivery`. The member `invoke` of a PHP class is its method `__invoke` (HYR-5), which the check accepts. `make recipe-check` reports 227 members that the spec does not hold, 28 fewer (H28.11).

- `Choice` and `ClientRendering` are classes with a constructor in a file of their own in the three servers, and `contracts/interfaces.json` declares their 7 members. Node declared them as interfaces in `app.ts` and now exports the classes from `choice.ts` and `client-rendering.ts`; an object with the same members is still accepted. The Python module `polyspec.hyper.client` is replaced by `polyspec.hyper.choice` and `polyspec.hyper.client_rendering`, whose classes declare their properties, and the package `polyspec.hyper` also exports `Choice`, `ClientRendering`, `MediaType`, `Representation` and `UploadedFile`. `make recipe-check` reports 255 members that the spec does not hold, 19 fewer (H28.11).

- The units that only the PHP and Python servers have (manifest, region, router, reads and renderer, HY-54.3) have one form, and `contracts/interfaces.json` declares their 44 members. The module functions of `_manifest.py`, `_router.py` and `_reads.py` are static methods of their classes, as in PHP, so `strip_base_path` is `Router.strip_base_path`. `Manifest` and `Region` of Python declare their properties, `Manifest` has `_region_name`, `Reads` has one `_keep_array` for a map and a list, and `Renderer` binds with a method, keeps `_render` private and loses `merge`, which nothing called. Four private members have names that no other member has: `keptKinds`, `namedKeys`, `mergedNode` and `compiledPath`. A rendering test of Python gave a header a lone surrogate to stand for a byte that is not UTF-8, which HY-42.6 now fails; it gives the byte. `make recipe-check` reports 274 members that the spec does not hold, 68 fewer (H28.11).

- The request has one form in the three servers, and `contracts/interfaces.json` declares its 41 members and the options record `RequestInit`. Node creates a request with `new Request(init)`, whose members are the named parameters of the PHP and Python constructors; `Request.from` and `selectedType` are removed, and the host function `requestOf` of `http.ts` turns the parts of `node:http` into those parameters. PHP takes `bodySize`, Python takes `json`, and the three name the selection `withMedia`. `wantsJson` of PHP fails for a request without a selection, as in Node and Python; it returned false. `path` of PHP is private, so the path is read with `path()` in the three. A path, a raw query or a header with a character above U+00FF fails the creation of a Node or Python request with `Error` or `ValueError` (HY-42.6); Node cut the character to its low byte, and Python failed later with `UnicodeEncodeError`. The steps of the three case runners turn the text of a case into bytes with a function that fails for such a character. `contracts/request.json` holds 9 cases: the texts of a request, and `file(name)` with the pairs of `files()`. HY-106.9 states the options record of Node, and `make recipe-check` accepts a member that takes its named parameters as one record. The operations document names the request buffering of nginx, which writes an upload once more. `make recipe-check` reports 342 members that the spec does not hold, 123 fewer (H28.11, H28.11-2).

- The units of the representations have one form in the three servers, and `contracts/interfaces.json` declares their 27 members. An entry of a route is the unit `representation entry`, a class with a constructor in a file of its own: PHP held it as an array, Node as an interface and Python as a data class in the file of another unit. `MediaSelection` and `RepresentationEntries` of Python are classes with static methods, as in PHP and Node, and the Node selection returns the list of three values that the others return, without the type `Selected`. `MediaType` of Node has the method `parameters()`, as PHP and Python have; it was a property, and HY-106.1 no longer states that difference. `Representation` of Python declares its three properties.
