Five private members have names that no other member of the package has: `servedParameters`, `answeredMethods`, `varyNames`, `cachePolicy` and `sentLocation`. The PHP signatures of 35 members are those that PHP 8.4.27 reports; they were recorded with PHP 8.5, which writes `self` and `readonly` otherwise. `make recipe-check` reports 465 members that the spec does not hold, 60 fewer (H28.11).

- The board example uploads a file with a new post in its PHP, Node and Python servers (HY-131): the form of `board/create.tpl` is `multipart/form-data` with the file field `attachment`, the three applications open with both form types, and the action stores the name and the size of the file with the post and moves the file with `moveTo()` to `<database>.uploads/<id>`. The post page shows the name and the size, and the representation of the post holds them as `attachment`, or null without a file. `Posts` adds the two columns to a table that an earlier version created. A send step of `scripts/check-parity.mts` uploads a file in a multipart form and requires that every server stored its bytes, and the parity requests upload a file whose content holds a boundary line. The browser tests attach a file to the post that they create under server and client rendering. `make parity`, `make server-parity` and `make server-parity-python` pass, and `make e2e` passes 17 tests (H29.6-1).

- The board example has a context and a representation in its PHP, Node and Python servers: the context handler gives the name of the site, which `title.tpl` reads as `context.site.name`, and the route `board.show` declares `application/vnd.board.post+json` with `version` `1`, whose handler answers the post as JSON, or 404 without a post. `scripts/check-parity.mts` has the step `representation`, which sends a request with the `Accept` of the step to every server and requires the status of the step, no cookie and equal responses, checks the strong `ETag` of a representation against its `Content-Type` and body, and sets the value of the member `context` into the shared data before the browser code renders, as the browser does (HYC-22). The parity requests hold five representation steps, and `tests/e2e/board.spec.ts` requests the post as JSON from the server-rendered and the client-rendered board. `make parity`, `make server-parity` and `make server-parity-python` pass with 70 equal responses of each server, and `make e2e` passes 17 tests (H28.8).

- The three servers answer a `HEAD` request as the `GET` request of the same target (HY-27.4): every step runs with the method `GET`, and `App.handle` returns the `GET` response with its body. A `HEAD` of a page, of the shell and of `/_hyper/keep` received 405. The hosts write the status and the headers of such a response, with the `Content-Length` of its body, and no body (HY-27.5): the `http.server`, WSGI and ASGI hosts of Python wrote the body, and `App::run` of PHP wrote no `Content-Length`. Every 405 has `Allow` (HY-27.6): `GET, HEAD` for a route, `GET, HEAD, POST` for a route with an action, `GET, HEAD` for the shell path and `POST` for `/_hyper/keep`; only the 405 of a representation had it. `contracts/client.json` has five cases of them, and `tests/scripts/upload-servers.test.mts` sends a page, a representation, their `HEAD`, a request with `If-None-Match`, a `PUT` with JSON and a refused method to the three real servers over HTTP and requires the same status, headers and body (H28.9).

- The build fails for a layout that does not place `{# build}` exactly once, as for `{# title}` and `{# data}` (HY-125.7): `layout.tpl: places {# build} 0 times, expected once (HY-125)`. The layout of the board example and of the script fixtures `regions` and `chunks` place it; without it the board answered 500, because its program declared no definition `build` (H28.10).
- `make hyper-test-scripts` passes as a whole, 278 tests; 8 groups with 67 tests failed. The session worker of PHP gives `NativeSession` a request with the session cookie, which the class takes since the cookies are parsed once. The target runs the files `tests/scripts/*.test.mts` and no file below `tests/scripts/fixtures`, of which four were run as tests. `benchmarks/server.php` reads `JsonCodec` and the build id; it named the class `JsonEncoder`, which does not exist. The server and browser benchmarks, `scripts/check-parity.mts`, `tests/e2e/board.spec.ts` and the README of the Node server send and expect `application/vnd.hyper+json`; they named `application/json`, which the servers answer with 406 (H28.11-9).

- The build case runner runs again: `packages/server/ts/tests/case-reader.ts` imports `./case-rules.ts` by its file name, which Node resolves, and `packages/server/ts/tsconfig.json` allows that extension for the tests, which are not built. With `./case-rules.js` `tests/scripts/build-id.test.mts` ran no test and wrote no result (H28.11-7).
- `make packages` and `make template-ext` remove the staging directory of a build that fails or is interrupted (HR-87): the shell of the recipe line removes it when it ends. A failed build left `dist.next-<pid>` in its package each time. `tests/scripts/check-recipes.test.mts` runs `make packages` with a build that fails in the second package and requires that no staging directory is left (H28.11-8).

- The PHP server answers the representations of a route (HY-116 to HY-122), as the Python and Node servers do: `representations` in the handlers of a route, a handler that receives a `Request` and a `MediaType` by their parameter types, `new Representation(status:, body:, headers:)`, `MediaType::parameters()` and `etag()`, and `Request::json()`. The three case runners pass every case of the contracts, 689 of 689, and `make hyper-test-php`, `make hyper-test-node` and `make hyper-test-python` pass. The manifest file is JSON text as HY-54 decodes it in the three servers (HY-2.8): a key that starts with U+0000 and a lone surrogate escape fail the open, which the Python and Node servers accepted. A served value of a parameter holds only HTAB, SP and U+0021 to U+007E (HY-118.6); the servers accepted U+0080 to U+00FF, whose bytes in a header differ between the runtimes. `parameters` is a JSON object and the lists are JSON arrays (HY-118.7), which PHP reads from the objects of the manifest, because an array of PHP does not tell `{}` from `[]`. `Accept` has `best`, which gives the type of the highest weight with its weight, so that the selection of the page types is written once in each server. `tests/Support/RepresentationCalls.php` holds the handlers of the cases of PHP. The native template extension of the checkout was built again for PHP 8.4.27; the one in `build/ext` did not load in it (H28.7).

- The Node server answers the representations of a route (HY-116 to HY-122), as the Python server does: `representations` in the handlers of a route, a handler with the one argument `{ request, media, services }`, `MediaType` with `parameters` and `etag(body)`, `Representation`, and `json()` of the request. The server reads the entries from the manifest with their parameters in the order of the manifest, also for a parameter name that is an integer, and fails the open for an entry without a handler and for a handler of a type that the route does not declare. The manifest check of the Python and Node servers reads the type and the subtype of an entry as tokens before it changes them to lower case, so that a letter outside ASCII whose lower case is an ASCII letter does not pass, and both compare the directive names of `Cache-Control` after removing only spaces and tabs; a `WWW-Authenticate` of a handler that is not text fails in both. The steps of the Node case runner give the handlers of the cases and compare what a handler received. The Node runner passes every case of the contracts, 686 of 686 (H28.7).

- The Python server answers the representations of a route (HY-116 to HY-122). It selects the media type of a request among `text/html`, `application/vnd.hyper+json` and the declared types by the weights of `Accept`, with a declared type matched only by a range that names it with a served value of every required parameter, and answers 406 with the types that the path serves. A representation request opens no session and runs only its handler, which receives the request without its `Cookie`, with `json()` for the decoded body, and a `MediaType` with `parameters()` and `etag(body)`; the server answers 405 with `Allow`, 400 for a body of a `GET`, `HEAD` or `DELETE`, 415 and 400 for a body that is no JSON value of the data model, checks the status, the headers, the `Location` and the `Cache-Control` of what the handler returned, and gives a 200 of a `GET` or `HEAD` its strong `ETag` and a 304 to a request that names it. The step of the Python case runner gave its loader a `reply` without a class, so every page case of `contracts/representations.json` answered 500. The Python runner passes every case of the contracts, 686 of 686 (H28.7).

- `App::run` of PHP fails when `enable_post_data_reading` is on under a SAPI that serves requests, every SAPI but `cli` and `phpdbg` (HY-59.9); it failed only for an application whose form types include `multipart/form-data`. With the setting on PHP reads every request body into `$_POST` and `$_FILES` with its own reader before the application runs, which hyper never reads, so the code of that reader ran for every request with a body. `startPhp` of `scripts/board-servers.mts` and the servers of the PHP tests start PHP with the setting off, and `tests/scripts/upload-servers.test.mts` starts the server with it on and requires the 500 (H29.7).

- The results of `make bench-uploads` in `docs/operations/benchmark.md` are those of a run on PHP 8.4.27, the baseline; the first record was a run on PHP 8.5.10, which the `php` of the checkout named because it was the first of `PATH`. The PHP checks of the work on the request bodies were repeated on 8.4.27 with the same results: the 91 cases of `contracts/fields.json`, the unit tests, the comparison of the three servers and the upload over HTTP. `docs/operations/deployment.md` names the benchmark for the numbers and repeats only the memory of a request (H29.6-2).

- `config/rebuild.json` declares `benchmarks/uploads.mts`, `benchmarks/uploads-native.php` and `scripts/recipe/protocol.mts`, so `make rebuild-check` passes. The steps and the tests of the uploads hold each piece of code once: `filesOf` of the PHP fields steps gives the files with or without their sizes, and the tests of a multipart body of the WSGI and ASGI callables are the class `UploadCases` of `tests/transport.py`, which each takes with its own request; `make duplication-check` named them (H28.11-4).

- `.gitignore` no longer ignores the two tracked files of `tests/scripts/fixtures/recipe/vendor/`, the stand-in of the Composer autoloader of the recipe fixture: the rule `vendor/` matched them, so `tests/scripts/ignored-files.test.mts` failed (H28.11-4).

- `make layout-check` passes for the files of the recipe and of the upload tests: `scripts/contract-programs.mts` is TypeScript, as the infrastructure under `scripts/` is (it was `contract-programs.mjs`), and `config/layout.yml` declares with their reasons the files that stay in another language: the readers `scripts/recipe/read-php.php` and `scripts/recipe/read_python.py`, the sources of the recipe fixture, and the workers and servers of `tests/scripts/fixtures/multipart-worker` and `tests/scripts/fixtures/upload-server` (H28.11-4).

- `make bench-uploads` measures an upload in one real server of each implementation, and `make bench` runs it: the PHP application under PHP-FPM, called by a FastCGI client of `benchmarks/uploads.mts`, the Node server and the Python `http.server` server over HTTP, and the reader of PHP itself (`benchmarks/uploads-native.php`). It reports the time of a request, the processor time of the server and its largest memory for a file of 1 GiB, for 500 values with 500 files and for 900 values of repeated and bracketed names, and judges nothing. `docs/operations/benchmark.md` states the method and the results of one run, and `tests/scripts/benchmark-uploads.test.mts` runs the benchmark with a file of 1 MiB. The servers of `tests/scripts/fixtures/upload-server` answer the usage of their process with `UPLOAD_USAGE=1` and take their part limit from `UPLOAD_PARTS` (H29.6-2).

- The reader of a multipart body gives a chunk of the content of a part as it is when its last bytes hold no CR, which a delimiter that the next chunk completes starts with; it appended every chunk to its buffer and cut the content out of it again. Measured outside the repository, five runs each of a body of 1 GiB read from a file: the processor time fell from 768 ms (732 to 860) to 710 ms (673 to 803) in PHP, from 994 ms (965 to 1038) to 963 ms (925 to 1019) in Node and from 700 ms (676 to 718) to 673 ms (662 to 687) in Python. The ranges overlap, so the copy was a small part of the cost (H29.6-3).

- `docs/operations/deployment.md` has a section Uploads: the four limits of a request body with their options and defaults, the settings of the server in front that bound a slow client, the PHP settings that bound nothing when `enable_post_data_reading` is off, the temporary directory of a request with its removal and what a killed server leaves, and the measured memory and processor time of a body of 1 GiB in the three servers and in the reader of PHP (H29.6).

- `tests/scripts/upload-servers.test.mts` sends the same requests over HTTP to one real process of each server: `App::run` of PHP under its built-in server with `enable_post_data_reading` off, the server of `App.server` of Node and the `http.server` server of Python (HY-131). An upload of a file of 3 MiB with repeated names, a body without its closing delimiter, a form body and a request without a body give the same answer in the three servers; the file that the request hook moved holds the bytes that were sent, and the directory of a request is gone when PHP and the `http.server` server have closed the connection. No test reached the reading of `php://input` of `App::run` and the `http.server` transport before (H29.3-1).

- `tests/scripts/multipart-compare.test.mts` reads 314 request bodies with one real process of the PHP, Node and Python servers each and fails when a body gives another result in a server (HY-131): 300 bodies of a seeded generator, well formed, malformed and over a limit, each in chunks of its own size, and 14 named bodies, among them a file of 256 MiB at and over the file limit, 1000 parts at and over the part limit, text values at and over the value limit, and the forms of three defects of the multipart reader of PHP (a boundary longer than its buffer with its prefix in the content, CVE-2024-8925; a body of 100000 parts, CVE-2023-0662; a part header that never ends, CVE-2015-4024). It reports the time of each server and judges none. It found that the Node server removed the directory of a request while the stream of a file that it had destroyed was still opening, which failed with `ENOTEMPTY` in one of six runs: the server now removes the directory after its files are closed (HY-131.21), and an error of a stream that it destroyed is no failure of a write. A measurement of ten runs each showed why the Node server keeps its stream: with a write of each chunk before the next read, as in PHP and Python, every run stopped the event loop for more than 10 ms and up to 226 ms while it read 1 GiB, and with the stream none did (at most 4.5 ms) (H29.5).

- The application takes a value limit and a part limit (HY-131.7 to HY-131.12): `valueLimit` and `partLimit` in PHP and Node, `value_limit` and `part_limit` in Python. The value limit bounds a body that the server holds as one value and the names and contents of the text values of a multipart body together, and is the smaller of 8 MiB and the body limit when it is not declared; the part limit bounds the parts of a multipart body and is 1000. A value that is not a positive integer, or a value limit above the body limit, fails when the application opens. A body that passes one of them, or a file that passes the file limit, receives status 413 where a body over the body limit does, and every server stops reading it there and leaves no temporary file; a multipart body had no limit of its parts, and a file over the file limit was read to its end and refused after the form type and the CSRF token (HY-114.7). A request that is built from the bytes of a body is checked by its size, its parts and its files (HY-131.22). HY-131.20, which named the parts of the rule that no server implemented, is retired. Thirteen cases of `contracts/fields.json` hold each limit at its value and one above it (H29.4).

- Every host reads the body of a request in chunks (HY-131): `App::run` of PHP from `php://input`, the Node server from its request stream, and the WSGI callable, the ASGI callable and the `http.server` server of Python. A multipart body of an application with that form type goes into files of a directory of the request, which the host removes after the write hook and the disconnect hook returned, also for a request that fails; a file that the application moved stays. A request therefore no longer holds a multipart body or its files as values: PHP under `memory_limit = 8M` reads a body of 1 GiB. `Files.largest()` reads the size of a file and no longer its content. The Node server waits for the files of a body between two chunks, and its listeners leave the stream of a file with the first event; they collected on a long file. The tests of the Node server and of the WSGI and ASGI callables send a file of 1.5 MiB and a body without its end through the host and read the directory at the write hook and after it (H29.3).

- `RequestBody` reads the body of a request in chunks in the three servers (HY-131): a multipart body of an application with that form type goes through the reader, whose file parts are written to files of a new directory below the temporary directory of the system as they arrive, and `remove()` deletes that directory; another body is held as one value up to the body limit. `UploadedFile` gives `size()` and `moveTo(path)` (`move_to` in Python): a file moves once, a path that exists or a second move fails with the class of HY-46.2 and one message, and a path that is not a string fails with `TypeError`; `content()` reads the file and fails after a move. The Node server writes a file through a stream and waits for its buffer (HY-131.21). The section `streamed` of `contracts/fields.json` holds 13 cases of chunk sizes, the body limit, a malformed body, the moves and the removal. No host uses `RequestBody` yet, so a request still holds its body as one value (H29.3).

- The three servers read a multipart body with one state machine that takes the body in chunks (HY-131.2 to HY-131.6): `MultipartReader` finds the delimiters and holds between two chunks at most the header lines of one part or the delimiter, and `Multipart` extends it and collects the values and the files. A delimiter counts only at the start of the body or after a CRLF, so that `--boundary` inside a line of content is content; the servers cut the content there. A body without its closing delimiter, bytes other than CRLF or `--` after a delimiter, and a header line that starts with the delimiter fail the request with status 400; the servers read such a body without an error and dropped its parts. The parameters of a `Content-Disposition` and of a content type are read in order (HY-113.11, HY-113.13, HY-113.14): the text inside a quoted string is no parameter, so that `name="a; filename=x"` is a value and no file and a `boundary=` inside a quoted string is no boundary; a `name` without quotes is a name; `form-dataX` is not `form-data`; and a quoted boundary of no character is no boundary. Each multipart case of `contracts/fields.json` is also read in two chunks divided at every position, with the bytes that the reader holds checked against its bound, and 14 cases hold these inputs. The servers still hold the body and each file in memory (H29.2, H29.2-1).

- The PHP server reads fewer than four bytes between two delimiters of a multipart body as no part, as the Node and Python servers do; it read the rest of the body as the content of a part, because a negative length of `substr` counts from the end. `queryInt` of PHP refuses digits with a final line feed (`12%0A`), as Node and Python do. The patterns of the Python reader fold case in ASCII only, so that `multıpart/form-data` with U+0131 is no multipart type. The comments of `UploadedFile` state that a filename is as it was sent (HY-113.14). Two cases of `contracts/fields.json` hold the delimiters (H28.11).

- HY-131 specifies a `multipart/form-data` body read in chunks: the delimiter and the end of a body, the failure of a malformed body with status 400, the value limit and the part limit with status 413, the temporary file of a file part and its removal, and `size()` and `moveTo()` of `UploadedFile`. The rule states that no server implements it yet; each server reads a body as one value (H29.1).

- The three servers read a query, a form body and a multipart body alike (HY-56, HY-57, HY-113). `query()`, `form()` and `files()` of a request return a list of pairs of a name and its values (HY-56.7): PHP returned an iterable whose decimal names were integer keys, Node a `Map` and Python a `Fields` object. `file(name)` returns null (`None` in Python) for a name without a file; Node returned `undefined`. `UploadedFile` gives its four parts by the methods `name()`, `filename()`, `contentType()` (`content_type()` in Python) and `content()` in the three servers (HY-113.8); Node and Python gave them as properties. HY-57.10 and HY-113.11 to HY-113.15 state how a content type, a boundary, the header lines and the parameters of a part are read, and the servers follow them: a boundary `""` raised an error in Python and was no boundary in PHP and Node; a no-break space was a blank in PHP and not in Node and Python; a lone carriage return ended a header line in Node only; a filename that is not quoted and holds a `"` was the empty text in PHP; and a part that failed with HY-42 failed only its own half of the body in Node. A quoted filename keeps its backslashes (HY-113.3, HY-113.14): the servers removed each backslash, so that the filename `C:\dir\a.txt` was `C:dira.txt`. A part header longer than 8192 bytes fails the body with status 400 (HY-113.16): for a filename of 100000 escaped characters PHP dropped the file without an error, and for one of 5000000 Node failed with a stack overflow and Python read for one second. The cases of `contracts/fields.json` hold these inputs, and the native tests of the fields and the files of the three servers moved into them. `Fields.has` is removed, because no source called it. `make python-file-parts-test` runs the rendering cases only; its first check selected `test_files.py`, which no longer exists, and failed with no test (H28.11).

- The recipe readers read two more forms (HYR-3): a PHP class of static methods that extends another class or is abstract is a `class` unit, not a `functions` unit, and a Node module of functions is internal when each of its exported functions carries `@internal`; the reader reported every such module as public (H28.11).

- `docs/operations/dependencies.md` records the check of the multipart parsers again at their latest releases, `riverline/multipart-parser` 2.3.0 and `python-multipart` 0.0.33: each still differs from HY-57 or HY-113, so each server keeps its parser (H28.11).

- `notes()` of a reply returns a list of pairs of a name and its value in the three servers (HY-60.29): PHP returned an array whose decimal names were integer keys, so that the notes `"0"` and `"1"` were a list, while Node returned a `Map` and Python a `dict`. HY-106.8 states that a call with another number of arguments than the parameters of a public method is answered by the calling convention of each runtime, and the comments of the note name cite HY-60.28 (H28.11).

- The test of the installed packages gives `App.open` the build file that it requires since the Node server reads the build id when it opens (HY-125), so that `make typecheck` passes for `tests/package-install` (H28.11).

- The reply of a request fails alike in the three servers: a cookie name or value, a `Cache-Control`, a `Referrer-Policy` or a note name that is not a string, and a `Max-Age` or a page status that is not an integer of the data model, fail with `TypeError` and one message (HY-52.11, HY-60.28, HY-69.6, HY-103.8), and a value outside the rules fails with the class of HY-46.2 and one message (HY-52.12). Before, PHP converted an integer for a caller without strict types, Node accepted the number 5 as the cookie value `5`, Python raised its own `TypeError`, and Node used `Error` with the prefix `hyper:`. A `Max-Age` and a status take a float with an integer value, such as `60.0`, as the integer, as Node does (HY-52.10). `contracts/reply.json` holds 85 cases that the three runners pass, in place of the native reply tests, and the Python `Reply` keeps its state in private dataclass fields without a constructor, as PHP and Node do (H28.11).

- `contracts/interfaces.json` states the PHP signatures `flash(mixed $name, mixed $value)` and `changed(mixed ...$topics)` with the `TypeError` of HY-25.6, and PHP `Result::flash` checks a value in place of a private helper, so that `make recipe-check` reports no failure of the result concern (H28.11).

- The check of a private Python constructor also counts a call through an absolute import of the package, such as `from polyspec.hyper import MediaType`, as it counts a relative one (HYR-8). The task H28.11-1 is in progress again, because its runners pass only when H28.7 adds the representations and the client cases (H28.11).

- PHP `Result::flash` and `Result::changed` check a name and a topic themselves, so that a caller without strict types gets the `TypeError` of HY-25.6 in place of an integer name converted to a string; the three servers give the same messages, which the section `types` of `contracts/result.json` compares. `DataModel::contains` returns false only for an error with a message of HY-44.8, and passes another error, such as a stack overflow, to its caller (H28.11).

- The check of a private Python constructor follows the imports of a file: the Python reader records a call of an imported name by its original name and the module of its import, also through an alias or an attribute of an imported module, and the check counts only a call from the module of the class or from the package, so that a class of the same name in another module no longer fails. A constructor that the spec declares internal keeps the comparison of its visibility, and a Python method whose name starts with `__` fails as `MEMBER_NAME`, because a private method starts with one `_` (HYR-8) (H28.11).

- PHP `Result::flash` keeps a decimal flash name and the place of a name stored again, as HY-25.5 states: a spread numbered the names that PHP keeps as integer keys again, so that `flash('5', …)` lost its name. Node and Python fail a flash name or a changed topic that is not a string with `TypeError`, as PHP does by its declared types (HY-25.6), and `contracts/result.json` adds the section `types` and a case of decimal names. The data of a PHP result is declared as a map with any keys (HY-25) (H28.11).

- A value outside the data model fails with one error per kind in the three servers, of the classes of HY-46.2 (`InvalidArgumentException`, `RangeError`, `ValueError`), with the messages that HY-44.8 states; Node failed with the `BindError` of the template or a `TypeError`, PHP with the `BindError` of the template or an `UnexpectedValueException`, and the messages differed. Node and PHP keep the error of the binding as the cause, and `DataModel.contains` returns false only for that error and passes any other failure on, where Node and PHP returned false for every failure (HY-44) (H28.11-1).

- The results of actions and the stops of loaders and actions are one unit per file in the three servers (`Result`, `Redirect`, `NotFound`, `Forbidden`, `BadRequest`), and `contracts/result.json` holds their cases, which replace the native tests. The three servers fail a redirect location, a page status and a redirect stop of a result that is not a redirect with the same message and the classes of HY-46.2 (`InvalidArgumentException`, `RangeError`, `ValueError`); Node threw `Error` with other messages. PHP `Result::flash` fails a value outside the data model, as Node and Python did (HY-25.4). Python rejects a redirect location with a lone surrogate, as Node does, names the method that stores a flash value `flash` instead of `flash_value`, and imports the stops from the modules `redirect`, `not_found`, `forbidden` and `bad_request`. The stops carry the messages `not found`, `forbidden` and `bad request` in the three servers (HY-25, HY-27, HY-46, HY-50, HY-58) (H28.11).

- The recipe check takes the visibility of a Python constructor from the spec, because `__init__` has no form with `_`, and fails a call of a class whose constructor the spec declares private in another file of the package; it failed every private Python constructor as public. The Python reader records the calls of a name for this check, and the test types of the readers name the parameters of a member (HYR-8) (H28.11).

- The Python reader of the recipe check counts a variadic parameter (`*name`, `**name`) as an input, as the PHP and Node readers count `...$name` and `...name`; it left it out, so that a method with a variadic parameter differed from its inputs in the spec (HYR-8) (H28.11).

- The Python reader of the recipe check lists a class-level annotated attribute whose name starts with one `_` as an internal property, as HYR-8 states; it dropped every such attribute, so that an internal property of a public Python module could not be listed. An attribute whose name starts with `__` is private to its class and is not a member, and the Python `Container` keeps its factories and instances in such attributes (HYR-8) (H28.11).

- HY-106.6 names every failure of the build file, a missing or unreadable file, bytes that are not UTF-8 or JSON and a file without a build id, as the subclass `InvalidBuild` of the open error, and Node `Build.read` reports only a `TypeError` of the UTF-8 decoder as not valid JSON, so that a defect of the JSON decoder is not reported as invalid JSON (HY-106, HY-125) (H28.11).

- `Build.read` of the three servers decodes `build.json` with `JsonCodec.decode` of its server, so that one nesting limit and one error apply: Python raised a raw `RecursionError` for deep nesting, and PHP and Node answered such a file differently. HY-106.6 states that a build file that does not hold a build id raises `InvalidBuild`, the subclass of the open error. `contracts/build.json` adds a case with a byte that is not UTF-8 inside the build id and a case nested 600 deep. The context step of Node passes shared, loader and action data to the server as host values, as PHP and Python do, so that the server and not the step checks the data model (HY-125, HY-106, HY-54, HY-44) (H28.11).

- `Build.read` of the three servers fails a `build.json` whose bytes are not UTF-8 or that starts with U+FEFF as `is not valid JSON` and keeps the cause of each failure: Python reported bytes that are not UTF-8 as `is missing or not readable`, and Node replaced them with U+FFFD, so that such bytes inside the build id failed as `does not hold exactly the key build`. `contracts/build.json` writes the bytes of a file in its new field `bytes` and compares the cause of a failure in its new field `message`, and its steps fail for a `text` that is neither a string nor null instead of treating it as a missing file (HY-125, HY-106) (H28.11).

- The steps of the applications of the cases check each member by one rule in PHP, Node and Python: `page` is an integer and not a boolean, `data`, a loader and the shared data are maps, `redirect` is a string and `embedData` a list of strings. Node reads the application through a checked reader instead of a cast, and PHP reads it as an untyped array whose members are checked where they are read, so that the tests of the steps have no type error left; the one value that a case gives against the type of its handler, a context that is not a map and a host function that is not a function, is marked with `@ts-expect-error` and `@phpstan-ignore` with its reason (H28.11).

- The browser code keeps a second leading U+FEFF of an event stream as a character of its first line, as the standard of server-sent events requires: the decoder dropped one U+FEFF and the stream parser another, so that a line after two of them dispatched its event. HY-42.5 states that a leading U+FEFF of a decoded name, value, route parameter and cookie value is a character of it, and HY-106.7 that a file that starts with U+FEFF is not valid JSON; the PHP and Python open tests check such files, as the Node test does (H28.11).

- The unit `Csrf` of the three servers masks and verifies only with a token and a mask of 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits and fails for any other with the same message, as HY-24.9 now states: a token of 62 digits gave a value of 126 digits in all three, Python read upper case digits and white space, PHP warned, and Node threw at a stored token of another length while PHP and Python answered false. `xor` fails for bytes of two lengths instead of keeping the shorter. HY-24.9 also states that a session that holds another value in the place of the token gets a new token. `contracts/csrf.json` compares the error of a token of 62 digits, a mask with upper case digits and a mask with white space (HY-24) (H28.11).

- The Korean recipe (`docs/spec/recipe.ko.md`) states the rules of HYR-8, the field `keys`, the cookies and the white space as the English recipe does, after a review of the translation, and holds the hash of the reviewed English text (H28.11).

- The steps of `contracts/context.json`, `contracts/functions.json` and `contracts/representations.json` read an application by one rule in PHP, Node and Python: an absent `shared`, `actions`, `embedData` or `context` declares none, a present one must have its type, an action has `redirect` or an integer `page` with `data`, `stop` must be `NotFound`, and only a declared loader or action is registered; PHP no longer casts the value of a member, Node no longer registers an empty loader for every route or answers an action without `page` with 200, and the session token of a case comes from a first `text/html` request in every language. Python decodes the context of a case without NaN and Infinity, which PHP and Node refuse. The container step counts only the error `no factory is bound for` as the declared failure, and the Python csrf step fails for a field that is not a string, as PHP and Node do (H28.11).

- The Node server and the browser router keep a leading U+FEFF of a decoded cookie value, form or query field and route parameter as a character, as PHP and Python do; `TextDecoder` dropped it by default, so that `a=%EF%BB%BFx` gave `x`. A JSON file of a Node application that starts with U+FEFF is not valid JSON, as in PHP and Python, and the errors of an application file that is not valid UTF-8 or not valid JSON carry their cause. `contracts/cookies.json`, `contracts/fields.json` and `contracts/routes.json` hold a case of a leading U+FEFF (HY-42, HY-106, HY-129) (H28.11).

- The context is the unit `Context` in the three servers, with the static members `held`, `text`, `hash`, `member`, `validHeader` and `withContext`; the browser places the context with `withContext` of `@polyspec/hyper-client`. The browser places the context of a JSON response in `shared` at its place of HY-10.3 instead of at the end, and placing the context in shared data that already holds `context` fails. The Python server fails a shared handler that returns the key `context` also when the application has no context handler, as PHP and Node do (HY-124). The Node context handler reports the cause of a value outside the data model (HY-44) instead of a generic message, and the Node message of a shared handler that returns `context` reads `returned`, as in PHP and Python. PHP keeps the integer keys of the shared data when the context comes first. The Node type of the one argument of a loader and an action is `HandlerArgument`, which replaces `Context`, and `ContextHandler` is exported. The cases of `contracts/context.json` compare the value at the place of the context and the failure that the response hook receives (HY-60), add a context handler that returns a list, a context outside the data model and a shared handler that returns `context` in an application without context, and read their fields without a default (H28.11).

- The case steps of `contracts/container.json` and `contracts/csrf.json` in PHP, Node and Python fail for a service name that the cases do not declare and for a field that is not a string, instead of reading the name as `mailer` and the field as the empty text (H28.11).

- The client and Node server tests load a linked `@polyspec/template` as one module instance: vitest keeps the link path as the module id (`resolve.preserveSymlinks`), because it named the same file of the template by its link path for `engine.ts`, `keep.ts` and `templates.ts` and by its real path for `response.ts`, `hyper.ts` and `obtain.ts`, so that a map that `response.ts` bound failed in the engine with `assign is not a map` and `tests/functions.test.ts` failed (H28.11).

- The PHP host functions are typed `array<string, callable(): mixed>` from `App::open` to `Renderer::open`, as the template engine requires, and the context handler returns `array<string, mixed>`; PHPStan reports no error in `src/` of the PHP server, and Node `app.ts` no longer imports the unused type `Manifest` (H28.11).

- The warnings of a request become errors through the unit `WarningsGuard` with the static member `run(work)` in the three servers, which PHP `App` used to write inline. The regions of a response are selected by the unit `RegionPlanner` with the static members `changedTopics` and `select` in the three servers, in `region-planner.ts` and `_region_planner.py`. The Node services are the class `Container` of `container.ts`, which the package exports in place of `Services`, and the Python `Container` holds its factories and services as fields of a dataclass; the spec lists `call` and the private `service` for PHP and Python and `get` for Node (HY-105.4). The cases of the native container tests are in `contracts/container.json` (HY-74, HY-11, HY-18, HY-19, HY-105) (H28.11).

- `make recipe-check` takes the receiver of a Python method by its place, the first parameter of a method that is not static, and no longer drops a later parameter named `cls` or `self` from its inputs (H28.11).

- The build id is read by the unit `Build` with the static member `read(file)` in the three servers, which fails with the error unit `InvalidBuild` and the same message that names the path of the file; PHP `App` holds the build id as a string. The cases of the native build file tests are in `contracts/build.json` (HY-125.5) (H28.11).

- The spec lists the members of the unit `Response` of the three servers, and `send`, `sendReplacing` and `writeHeaders` for PHP alone (HY-100.30). The Python `Response` declares `status`, `headers` and `body` as attributes of the class, as PHP and Node declare them as properties (HY-130) (H28.11).

- `make recipe-check` names an internal member of a public Python module with a leading `_`, as HYR-5 states, and no longer reports it as `MEMBER_NAME` (H28.11).

- The JSON text is the unit `JsonCodec` in the three servers, with the static members `value`, `encode`, `decode` and `inDataModel`; PHP writes and reads JSON text with `json_encode` and `json_decode`, and Node and Python with the private members that the spec lists for them alone (HY-54.4, HY-54.8). `OutsideNumber` and `JsonDecodeError` are units of their own files. The data model is the unit `DataModel` with `check`, `contains` and the private member `walk` in the three servers, and `inDataModel` is `DataModel.contains`, so a decoded value that nests deeper than the data model allows is outside it in every server; Python checks the nesting with a stack, so 511 nested lists no longer exceed its recursion limit. The cases of the native JSON tests are in `contracts/json.json` (HY-17, HY-44, HY-54) (H28.11).

- The kept values are the unit `Kept` in the three servers, with the static members `apply` and `select` and the private members `replaceAt`, `conforms`, `mapEntries` and `valueKind`; Node no longer applies them through the browser package. The section `select` of `contracts/keep.json` holds the values that the server sends and the unchanged region data, and the PHP step gives each case as arrays and as stdClass maps; they replace the native kept tests of each server (HY-38) (H28.11).

- The masked session token is the unit `Csrf` in the three servers, with the static members `masked`, `mask`, `verify`, `bytes` and `xor`. The new mask of every masked value is the section `masked` of `contracts/csrf.json`, which replaces the native csrf tests of each server (HY-24) (H28.11).

- The parse of a Cookie header is the unit `Cookies` in the three servers, with the static members `parse`, `percentDecode` and `utf8`, and the spec lists them (HY-129) (H28.11).

- The Accept parser and the selection of a media type are the unit `Accept` in the three servers, with the same members, a range `MediaRange` with its weight in thousandths and the error `InvalidAccept`; their cases are `contracts/accept.json`, which replaces the native accept tests of each server (HY-116) (H28.11).

- `make recipe-candidates` derives the members of a unit that `contracts/interfaces.json` lists without members, from the sources of its files, with their neutral fields listed as hand-written (HYR-10) (H28.11).

- `contracts/interfaces.json` states the concerns of the three servers with their rules, case files and units; the members of each unit follow with the move of its concern. `docs/spec/recipe.md` drops its section "Concerns and units", and the background of wave 28 lists the decisions of that division (H28.11).

- The case reader of each server holds exactly the members of HYR-7, and the rules that it applies to a case are in one test unit beside it (`Support/CaseRules.php`, `case-rules.ts`, `case_rules.py`). `make recipe-check` counts a constructor as one member in every language, and a `sentence` may cite a statement of an HYC rule. The mutation cases fail a missing browser or build runner (H28.11).

- The reads cases of `contracts/reads.json` render their template with the kept data in every server: the PHP and Python runners build one program per case through `scripts/contract-programs.mts` (`make contract-programs`) and check that the kept data and the data render the same bytes (HY-73.28); the `only` of the reads `template`, `templates` and `render` fields is removed, and `reads` keeps its `only` (HY-73.23). `a_list_literal_reads_its_items_whole` reads `l[1]`, which the server type check accepts (H28.11).

- The build of a server application keeps the inputs of a template that a block or an include places with arguments, which `deriveTypeManifest` derives from those arguments (HY-73.16); only the targets, the layout, the title, the reserved data template and the route and region templates render with no template inputs (HY-12, HY-13, HY-30, HY-31) (H28.11).

- The case files name for each field the statement of `contracts/protocol.json` whose behaviour it checks, its comparison and its encoding (`caseFiles` of `contracts/interfaces.json`); response fields take their comparison from their name (HYR-11); each case that states a response lists the rules of that response in `requirements`. The PHP, Node, Python, browser and build runners read the cases through their case reader, and `make recipe-check` counts all five results files. The cases check three behaviours that native tests had checked: a request that opens no session calls no session store and sets no cookie (HY-120), the names of urlencoded form values in order (HY-56), and a route parameter named `__proto__` (HY-5, HY-49) (H28.11).

- An application that fails to open because a file that it reads is not valid JSON or not valid UTF-8 raises the open error of its runtime, `InvalidArgumentException` in PHP, `Error` in Node and `ValueError` in Python (HY-106); the browser router keeps a route parameter named `__proto__` (H28.11).

- `make recipe-check` reads the protocol statements, rules, terms and statuses from `contracts/protocol.json`, checks every `sentence` and `requirements` reference, and fails a case file that `caseFiles` does not declare (H28.11).

- `docs/spec/recipe.md` names its cases through `contracts/protocol.json` statement ids and states the case comparisons in HYR-11: each expected field and step result names one `compare` kind, and `CASE_EMPTY` fails a case that compares nothing for its runner. The recipe names the runners and case readers through the JSON fields `runners` and `caseReaders` instead of file names, and `only` names one statement with its `sentence` (H28.11).

- `contracts/protocol.json` is the one source of the protocol: every rule is a list of statements, one English sentence each with its Korean translation and the sha256 of the English that the translation follows, and `docs/spec/protocol.md` and `protocol.ko.md` are generated from it with an anchor per statement. The Korean follows the English statement by statement, and the conversion corrected the Korean where it said something other than the English (for example constant time in HY-24, the layout of the response in HYC-2, the referent of the context hash in HY-125). AGENTS.md names the JSON as the place of the protocol rules (H28.11).

- `contracts/protocol.json` retires the statements that the case text of HY-130 and the removed rules HY-9, HY-41 and HY-123 held, moves HY-130 to the section of request boundary and failures, and gives the interface terms their own section; the test comments, the feature table, the execution plan, the checklist, the recipe and the app headers now cite the statement ids and rule ids that hold each case (H28.11).

- `make spec-docs` writes `docs/spec/protocol.md` and `docs/spec/protocol.ko.md` from `contracts/protocol.json`; `make spec-docs-check` checks both documents against that rendering, and the CI `docs` group runs it (H28.11).

- `make recipe-check` reads `contracts/interfaces.json` and checks it against the PHP, Node and Python sources and their case results (HYR-7, HYR-8); `make recipe-candidates` writes the candidate of that spec under `var/recipe` with its hand-written fields listed beside it (HYR-10) (H28.11).

- The owner rule covers `scripts/recipe`, `scripts/schema` and the recipe fixtures; the Node reader pins TypeScript 6.0.3 in its own private package, recorded in docs/operations/dependencies.md (H28.11).

- `contracts/representations.json` states its application under `applications`, with its manifest, templates, loaders, base path and build id, as the other contracts do (H28.1-9).

- `contracts/functions.json` holds the cases of the host functions of HYC-24: a document that renders a registered function, the 500 of a call of a name that is not registered, and the open that a built-in name or an entry that is not a function fails (H28.1-8).

- HY-97, HY-100, HY-121 and HY-123 and `contracts/representations.json` name the entry of a request `App.handle`, as HY-106 names the public entry of every server (H28.1-7).

- `contracts/context.json` expects only `title` in the shared data of a JSON response whose templates read no other shared value (HY-73), and the case of a loader that stops lists the shared handler after the context handler; HY-128 states how a runner masks the token of its session and compares `firstCalls` (H28.1-6).

- `contracts/client.json` holds again the cases that the servers of main pass; the client cases of wave 28 land with the servers that implement them (H28.1-5).

- docs/spec/protocol.md names the PHP, Node and Python servers in every sentence that said both servers: the cases of `contracts/fields.json`, `contracts/csrf.json`, `contracts/reads.json` and `contracts/json.json` and the HY-44 failure of an application object, which a Python rendering test now states for a `datetime` (H28.1-4).

- docs/spec/protocol.md corrects the rules of wave 28 by two audits of their text: the media type is selected once and held by the request, an invalid `Accept` receives 400, the 406 body lists every combination of the served parameter values, and every response of a representation request after the selection has one `Vary` (HY-116, HY-117); the manifest rejects page types, wildcards, `q` and the `vary` names `Accept` and `Cookie` (HY-118); the application opens the session after the checks of hyper and before the token check (HY-120); a `GET`, `HEAD` or `DELETE` with a body receives 400, a JSON body decodes as HY-54 decodes kept values, and `HEAD` returns the `GET` response through `App.respond` (HY-121); a `Location` is an application path or an absolute URI (HY-122); the context handler runs after the action and before the shared handler and the loaders (HY-124), the context is checked on every call and encoded only for a response that holds it (HY-125), and `Hyper-Context` is checked before the token check (HY-127); the public API of the context handler, the host functions and the representation handlers is named for each runtime (HY-106). `contracts/representations.json`, `contracts/context.json` and `contracts/client.json` hold the cases of these rules (H28.1-3).

- The asset build writes `build.json` with the build id, the SHA-256 digest of the paths and bytes of the outputs that the browser runs, and the element `hyper-build` of the static shell; the server build requires `--build`, the path of that file, validates it and writes it into the program directory (HY-125) (H28.6).

- docs/spec/protocol.md names the path of each output that the build id hashes: a file below `public/assets` by its URL path without the leading slash, and `manifest.json` and `templates.index.json` by their names in the directory of `--output` (HY-125); the build cases of `contracts/context.json` use those paths (H28.1-2).

- docs/spec/protocol.md states that the server build takes the `build.json` of the asset output with its option `--build`, writes it into the program directory and fails for a missing file or an invalid build id (HY-125) (H28.1-1).

- docs/spec/protocol.md specifies the selection of the media type by `Accept` after routing with its `Vary` (HY-116, HY-117), the media type `application/vnd.hyper+json` of the page data (HY-15), the representations of a route with their own media types, methods and response rules, which read no session and no cookie (HY-118 to HY-123), the context of an application that a JSON response holds only when its hash changed, with the `Hyper-Context` header and the build id in `build.json`, in every JSON response and in the definition `build` (HY-124 to HY-128), and in the browser the held context, a new document for another build, the latest region response and the host functions (HYC-22 to HYC-24); `contracts/representations.json` and `contracts/context.json` hold their cases and `contracts/client.json` follows the new media type (H28.1).
