- The template repository is used at its tag `v0.0.2`, the released tag of the template repository (H13.5-7, HY-80). `TEMPLATE_TAG` of the Makefile is `v0.0.2`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `.github/workflows/release.yml` check out the template repository at `v0.0.2`, and the documents name that tag. `ci-workflow.test.mjs` and `template-copy.test.mjs` read the tag from `TEMPLATE_TAG` of the Makefile. The template tag `v0.0.1` has no GitHub Release. `make install` copies the commit of `v0.0.2`, and `make test-js`, `make test-node` and `make test-php` pass on that copy.
- The notes of a GitHub Release fit the body limit of GitHub, 125000 characters (H13.5-6, HY-95). `make release-publish` passes the section `## X.Y.Z` of `CHANGELOG.md` when it has at most 125000 characters, and otherwise the one line `The changes of X.Y.Z are listed in [CHANGELOG.md](https://github.com/polyspec/hyper/blob/<tag>/CHANGELOG.md#<anchor>).`; the anchor is the id of an `<a id="...">` line above the heading, or else the version without its dots, and each segment of the tag is URL-encoded. GitHub refuses a release whose body is longer than 125000 characters.

## 0.0.1

### Added

- The release covers a Go module tag at any depth, and its assets are npm tarballs and Composer zips only (H13.5-4, HY-95). The trigger of `.github/workflows/release.yml` is `tags: ['v*', '**/v*']`: in a tag filter `*` does not match `/`, so `**/v*` covers the tag of a Go module at any depth. A Cargo package is not released as an archive; it is consumed by git tag, because `cargo package` rewrites git dependencies into crates.io requirements that do not resolve. `scripts/release.mjs` lists a tracked `Cargo.toml` with that reason, and `tests/scripts/release.test.mjs` fails on another asset kind.
- A tag of a commit of `main` releases it (H13.5-3, HY-95). `.github/workflows/release.yml` runs on the push of a tag `v*` or `**/v*` with the permission `contents: write`; `make release-verify` requires the tagged commit on `origin/main` with the check runs `push-gate` and `ci-passed` concluded `success`, `make release-versions` the version of the tag in every manifest and the section `## X.Y.Z` in `CHANGELOG.md`, `make release-assets` builds the packages and the npm and Composer archives, and `make release-publish` creates the GitHub Release with the section as notes (`scripts/release.mjs`, `tests/scripts/release.test.mjs`). AGENTS states the release procedure.
- The ruleset `main` requires exactly the checks `push-gate` and `ci-passed` (H13.5-2, HY-94). `ci-passed`, the last job of `ci.yml`, needs every other job, runs after each of them under `if: ${{ always() }}` and runs `make ci-passed`, which fails unless every needed job has the result `success`; a job added to `ci.yml` is required once it is in `needs`. `ci-workflow.test.mjs` fails when the job is missing, is not last, lacks `if: ${{ always() }}`, does not need every other job, runs on another runner or runs another step.
- Both changelogs keep the section `## Unreleased` at the top, and every change adds its entry there (H13.5-1). No version of this repository is released, so every entry is in that section; the release pull request renames it to `## X.Y.Z`.
- The workflow `ci.yml` also runs on a manual run (`workflow_dispatch`), and each workflow declares exactly its triggers (H13.4-1, HY-91, HY-94): `ci.yml` runs on `pull_request`, `merge_group` and `workflow_dispatch`, and `push-gate.yml` on `push` outside `gh-readonly-queue/**`, `pull_request` and `merge_group`. `ci-workflow.test.mjs` requires the `on:` block of each workflow exactly and no other workflow.
- Every change reaches `main` through a pull request and the merge queue (H13.4, HY-94). The ruleset `main` requires a pull request without approval that allows every merge method, the merge queue with the method `REBASE`, a linear history and the checks `push-gate`, `check (docs)`, `check (php)`, `check (node)` and `check (board)`, and refuses a force-push and a deletion without a bypass actor; the declaration also sets `allow_rebase_merge`, `allow_auto_merge` and `delete_branch_on_merge`. `ci.yml` runs on pull requests and merge groups, no longer on a push to `main`, and cancels only pull request runs; `push-gate.yml` also runs on merge groups and skips the branches of the queue.
- The job `push-gate`, the check that the ruleset `main` requires, runs `make docs-check` after the gate of tasks (H13.2, HY-94). It checked only for a task in progress, so a commit whose checklist or documents broke the rules of `make docs-check`, such as a state marker outside a task state, passed it and could reach `main`. The document checks read no network.
- The browser obtains the data that it does not hold with one document request for the URL of the current page (H15.2, HY-93). After H15.1 a server-rendered page without embedded data held nothing, so `hy-set`, `set`, `render` and the browser kept values of its route regions failed. `Hyper.open()` starts a page: a server-rendered page holds its embedded data, or requests the data at load only when a `localStorage` or `sessionStorage` value is stored for a route region of its route, so a page that is only viewed sends no request beyond its document. The first change of a route region requests the data once, renders every present route region from it and then applies the change; `data(region)` returns a promise. A region response holds all that the browser holds, so the server needs no signal of the held data. The entry templates no longer hold `hyper/data.tpl`. 14 cases of `hyper.test.ts` failed before the change; 229 browser cases and 335 Node cases pass after it.
- A document embeds its data only when the reply of its request asks for it with `Reply::embedData()` in PHP or `reply.embedData()` in Node (H15.1, HY-92, HY-31). Every server-rendered document of a route with a present route region embedded its data in `#hy-data`, so the source of a page showed its data next to its HTML and the HTML was larger. `renderDocument` of the browser package takes `{ embed }`; the Node server passes the choice of the reply, and a client-rendered document never embeds its data, because the browser holds the data of the JSON that it rendered. JSON responses do not change. The board example embeds the data of the post page and none on the list. `AppTest` failed 6 cases with 500 before the change, because `Reply::embedData` did not exist, and `app.test.ts`, `client.test.ts`, `response.test.ts` and `hyper.test.ts` failed 9 cases; the 318 PHPUnit, 335 Node and 217 browser cases pass after it.
- The full suite runs on GitHub after a push to `main` and for every pull request (H14.14, HY-91). Only the push gate ran on GitHub, and the full suite ran only locally. The workflow `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs one job per CI group of the Makefile (`docs`, `php`, `node`, `board`) with `fail-fast: false`; the groups together run every target of `CHECK_TARGETS` once. Each job takes Node.js, the PHP minor and the template branch from the pinned files, builds the template package of that branch, runs `make install`, runs every target of its group to its end with `make ci-check` (`scripts/ci-run.mjs`), writes the job summary with `make ci-summary` and uploads the report `var/ci/<group>/` with the status, the time, the first failure lines and the full log of each target. Only the group `board` installs Chromium (`make install-browser`).
- A push is refused while a task of `docs/plans/execution-checklist.md` is `[~]` in a pushed commit or in the working tree (H13.1). AGENTS allowed a push only without a task in progress, and nothing enforced it. The tracked pre-push hook `.githooks/pre-push` runs `node scripts/push-gate.mjs hook`, which parses each checklist with `activeItems` of the guard of the full run, names each active ID with its task, and also refuses a pushed commit without the checklist. Every `make` run sets `core.hooksPath` to `.githooks`; `make hooks-check`, which `make docs-check` runs, and the guard of the full run fail while it is not set. The job `push-gate` of the new workflow `.github/workflows/push-gate.yml` runs `node scripts/push-gate.mjs commit <sha>` on every push and pull request and fails for a task in progress and for a commit that does not track the hook with mode 100755. Before the change `tests/scripts/push-gate.test.mjs` failed in its 6 cases with `ENOENT` for `.githooks/pre-push` and the workflow, and 3 cases of `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` failed for a checkout without the hook; after it the 17 cases of both files pass, among them a push to a temporary bare repository that is refused with the task in the pushed commit or only in the working tree and leaves the remote branch unchanged, and that succeeds without a task in progress.
- `make check` starts the guard of the full suite `scripts/full-run.mjs` before any step (H11.1-1). AGENTS states that `make check` runs exactly once, when every active task is done, and nothing enforced it: `make check` started its first step with a task in progress, with uncommitted changes and on a tree it had already verified. The guard prints its decision with the reason and refuses while a task of `docs/plans/execution-checklist.md` is `[~]`, listing each active ID with its task, while tracked changes are uncommitted, and when `var/full-run.json` records a full run of the current tree, naming that run. It runs each target of `CHECK_TARGETS` with `make <target>` to its end and writes the record before and after each target, so a stopped run stays `incomplete`. `make rerun-failed` reruns only the targets of the current tree that did not pass and completes the result when they pass. `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` failed with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before the change, while `make -n check` printed the first step of `template-check`; its 10 cases pass with stub targets after it.
- The asset build only adds files below `public/assets` whose names hold a hash of their content and writes the index, the manifest and the static shell `csr/` into the directory of the required option `--output` (H10.3, HY-34, HY-76). It removed the scripts and the template files of the earlier build and wrote its unhashed outputs into `public/` and `dist/`, so a server that still served the earlier build, or a page open in a browser, loaded files that were gone. The client imports the index as `@polyspec/hyper/templates-index`, which the build resolves to the index of the same build, because the index no longer lies at a path that the application names. The static deployment copies no stylesheet, because `public/assets` keeps the stylesheets of earlier builds and the copy would make the deployment depend on them; `make assets` copies the stylesheets of the board example. `tests/scripts/build-assets.test.mjs` failed with `Unknown option '--output'` before the change and passes now; `make assets` builds the board example into `examples/board/build`.
- The browser and Node packages call the host binding of the template package as `bindValue` (H10.5). Template T14.2 renamed it from `bind`, which now creates a bound map, so `tsc` of `packages/hyper-js` failed with `BoundMap is not assignable to Value` against template main and passes with the new name; `tsc --noEmit` of `packages/hyper-node` passes too.
- The selection of a client rendering returns a choice, whether the request is of a client-rendered page and a value, and every loader and action of the request reads the value with `$request->selection()` in PHP and `request.selection()` in Node (H9, HY-62). PHP has `new Choice(chosen: <bool>, value: <value>)`, and Node the type `Choice` `{ chosen, value }`, whose `value` is not `undefined`; a selection that returns `true` or `false` alone fails with HY-43. A request of an application without client rendering has the value null. The selection returned only `true` or `false`, so an application whose selection reads stored data, such as the service of the `Host` header, read the same rows again in its handlers in every request. `ClientTest` and `client.test.ts` failed before the change: `Choice` and `selection()` did not exist.
- The asset build compiles the Tailwind stylesheet before the static deployment copies the stylesheets (H8.2-1, HY-77). It wrote the output after the copy, so `dist/csr/assets/` missed the output of a first build and held that of the earlier build after a later one; `tests/scripts/build-assets.test.mjs` now compares the copy with the output and failed with `ENOENT` before the change.
- The asset build compiles an application stylesheet with Tailwind CSS when `--tailwind <source>=<output>` names it (H8, HY-77): the output declares the layers `theme`, `base`, `components` and `utilities`, holds the Tailwind theme and the utility classes that the files below `templates/` and `client/` use, and holds the rules of the source unchanged in the layer `components`, without a rule of the layer `base`. Tailwind CSS, `@tailwindcss/node` and `@tailwindcss/oxide` 4.3.3 are development dependencies of the repository, because the asset build of this repository compiles the stylesheet with them. `tests/scripts/build-assets.test.mjs` failed with `Unknown option '--tailwind'` before the change.
- The execution checklist uses four task states, `[ ]` waiting, `[~]` in progress, `[o]` done and `[!] cause: <cause>; retry: <condition>` bypassed (H7.1). `make docs-check` accepts no other state and requires the same states in both languages; it failed on the 23 `[x]` rows of each language before they were written as `[o]`. AGENTS states the checklist rules.
- A page requests only the resources that it uses, each once (H6, HY-76). The asset build writes code that the client entry imports with `import()` only into chunk files `public/assets/hyper-chunk-<hash>.js`, which the browser loads by their absolute URL when that code first runs, also from the entry that the static shell inlines; the entry is `public/assets/hyper-<hash>.js`, named by the hash of esbuild. It wrote all client code into one file, so an application could not load code that only some pages use when such a page needs it. `public/assets/manifest.json` holds only the entry URL `hyper`; it held `css` and `reader`, the stylesheet names of the board example, which another application does not have, and the board layout now links `/assets/app.css` and, on a post page, `/assets/reader.css` itself. The static shell contains no stylesheet; it inlined `app.css`, which the rendered layout links too, so a client-rendered page held that stylesheet twice. The build no longer requires `public/assets/app.css`. `tests/scripts/build-assets.test.mjs` failed before the change with `ENOENT` for `public/assets/app.css`, and the CSR stylesheet flow of `make e2e` failed with one `style` element. The header comments of `scripts/build-assets.mjs` and `scripts/build-templates.mjs` and item 2 of the asset build in `docs/operations/development.md` described the index with referenced templates, which H5 removed and did not update. The CSR shell limit falls from 34,000 to 33,200 gzip bytes, because the shell no longer inlines `app.css` and measures 32,782 gzip bytes.
- The browser loads a template when rendering reaches it (H5, HY-35). It renders, loads the templates that the rendering requested and did not hold, by their index URLs, and renders again. The index maps a name only to its URL (HY-34). A server-rendered page loads no template when it opens. The browser loaded every template that the templates of a route reference by path, transitively, before rendering, so a route whose template selects one of many views loaded every view. A template of a branch that the data does not select is now not loaded. The Node server reads every template of the index when it opens. Three cases of `hyper.test.ts` failed before the change: a region response and a client-side rendering of `/pick` loaded both `pick-a.tpl` and `pick-b.tpl`, and opening a server-rendered page loaded six templates. HY-30 now states that a change of a route region sends no request to the application and may load the template files that it renders; `make e2e` checks that the first changes of a server-rendered board load only `board-notice` and `board-rows` and that a client-rendered board loads none. The Node server fails to open when an entry template of a route is not in the index. `templateReferences` and its test are removed.
- A route region loader may return `null`, which makes the region absent from that response (H4, HY-75): it has no entry in `regions`, in the embedded data or in `kept`, and the page region receives no definition for it, so a template places a region that may be absent inside `{?# name}…{/}`. A route region may be placed in a template that the route template includes or places by path, and `make templates-check` follows those templates. The browser holds only present route regions, refuses `data`, `render`, `set` and `hy-set` for an absent one, and marks the body with `hy-error="0"` when a present route region has not exactly one element or an absent one has an element. A page that places views at request time under one route, such as a page that an administrator composes, can now declare a route region for a view that only some pages place. Every route region was rendered for every request of its route. The board example shows the notice on the first page only, and `make server-parity` compares `/board?page=2` after eleven posts. `AppTest`, `app.test.ts`, `hyper.test.ts`, `response.test.ts` and `check-templates.test.mjs` failed before the change.
- `make check` runs `make analyse-php`, which runs PHPStan 2.2 at level `max` on the source and the tests of `packages/hyper-php` without a baseline and without ignored errors (H3.3). The first run on "Fail a PHP request on a warning, notice or deprecation" reported 285 errors: 83 in `src` (`Manifest` 26, `Reads` 14, `Kept` 11, `App` 10, `Renderer` 5, `Csrf` 5, `Session` 4, `Request` 4, `Container` 2, `JsonEncoder` 2) and 202 in `tests` (`AppTest` 79, `ClientTest` 36, `RouterTest` 30, `FieldsTest` 18, `RunTest` 11, `KeptTest` 10, `ReadsTest` 8, `CsrfTest` 7, `Support/warnings.php` 2, `JsonTest` 1). The source now checks every decoded value before it uses it: the manifest, `reads.json` and `program.json`, the flash data of the session and the result of a service factory. The renderer holds a `Polyspec\Template\Program` or the native `Engine`. The data maps that loaders return have the type `array<array-key, mixed>`, because PHP stores a decimal key as an integer. A client rendering selection is a `\Closure(Request): mixed` whose result the server checks (HY-62). The tests read fixtures and responses through `tests/Support/Json.php`, which fails a test on a value of another type or a missing key.
- While the PHP server answers a request, a warning, notice or deprecation, such as an undefined variable or array key, becomes an exception and the request fails with the plain 500 of HY-43 (H3, HY-74). PHP continued with `null` and sent a wrong page with status 200, while the message reached only the log. An error that `@` silences stays silent, and the handler is removed after the request. PHPUnit lowers `error_reporting()` while a test runs, so `RunTest` runs `tests/Support/warnings.php` in a separate PHP process; it answered 200 with `"id":null` before the change.
- The server sends only the data that the templates of a route read (H2, HY-73). The browser package computes the read paths of every route from the template ASTs: variables, members, literal indexes, loops, assignments, includes and blocks follow paths, and an output, a test, an operand or an argument reads its value whole. `scripts/build-server.mjs` writes them to `reads.json` of the server program, which `App::open` of PHP now requires (HY-48), and the Node server computes them when the application opens. Before rendering or encoding a page, both servers keep only those paths of the shared data and of every region's data; a list keeps its length and an emptied map stays a map. Every loader value was sent, so a value that the page did not show, such as an internal identifier or a whole message catalog, reached the browser in every JSON response. The data model check of HY-44 now runs on every value that the handlers return before the removal, so an unread value outside the data model still fails. `conformance/reads.json` holds 21 cases that the browser package computes and renders and that `ReadsTest` keeps; `ReadsTest`, `RendererTest`, `AppTest` and the Node `app.test.ts` failed before the change. The `add` route of the test fixtures renders `add.tpl` with the CSRF field, and the token of the tests comes from `/list`, whose route region keeps a value on the server.
- The CSRF protection no longer depends on the application (H1). Every response carries `csrf` as a masked value of the session token: 32 random bytes drawn for that response followed by those bytes XOR the token, as 128 hexadecimal digits, and an action or a save of a kept value is accepted only with a masked value of the token (HY-24); `conformance/csrf.json` holds the cases that both servers pass. The token was the same in every response of a session, so the compressed size of a response that also contained request input, such as the path in `params`, could reveal it. The session cookie is `__Host-hy-session` on HTTPS and `hy-session` otherwise, with `Path=/` and no `Domain`, and the application no longer names it: PHP sets the name in `NativeSession`, and `FileSessions` of Node has no `name` option (HY-45). A cookie without the `__Host-` prefix could be set by a sibling subdomain, which then knew the token of the session that it gave the visitor. An action calls `renewSession()` of its reply at sign-in and sign-out; the server moves the session to a new identifier, deletes the old one and creates a new token, and a loader that calls it fails with 500 (HY-72). `Reply::cacheControl` and `reply.cacheControl` refuse a value without `private` or `no-store` or with `public` or `s-maxage`, because a shared cache would give the token of one visitor to another (HY-52). The `ETag` of a JSON response is weak and hashes the body with the masked token replaced by the session token, so the mask does not prevent a 304 and a renewed session has another tag (HY-53). A stored token that is not 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits is replaced. `CsrfTest`, `ReplyTest`, `NativeSessionTest`, `AppTest`, `csrf.test.ts`, `sessions.test.ts` and `app.test.ts` failed before the change; `make server-parity` compares the masked tokens with the weak tags.
- Embed only the data that the browser can change. A document embeds `env`, `route`, `params`, `shared`, the data of the route regions of its route and their kept entries; a route without route regions embeds no `#hy-data` (HY-31). Before, every document embedded the whole document JSON, so the source of every page showed the data of every region and all shared data, although the browser changes only route regions (HY-30). `data`, `render`, `set` and `hy-set` work only on the route regions of the held route (HY-33, HY-36), the browser holds only their data (HY-32), and the manifest check rejects `keep` on a manifest region (HY-37). JSON responses keep every region. The new rule HY-71 states that every value the server sends reaches the browser, so loaders return only values that the visitor may see. PHP, Node and browser tests and an e2e test check the embedded data.
- The response hook also receives the failure of a 500 of HY-43 or HY-66 as its fifth argument, `?string $failure` in PHP and `failure: string | null` in Node (HY-60): the message of the error, or `the response to <method> <path> has <size> bytes, more than the response limit of <limit> bytes`; it receives null for every other response. The server wrote the cause only to its own log, so an application could not write it on its own line of the request. `AppTest` and `app.test.ts` failed before the change.
- `scripts/build-assets.mjs` and `scripts/build-templates.mjs` require `--template-dir`, as `scripts/build-server.mjs` does, and read the parser, the render runtime and the manifest check bundle from the template package `packages/template-ts` of that template repository; the asset build also resolves `@polyspec/template` in the client bundle to that package (HY-70). They resolved `@polyspec/template` from the dependencies of this repository, which link the template checkout next to it, so a rebuild of that checkout removed the package while a build of an application read it, and an application could not build with another template package. `tests/scripts/template-dir.test.mjs` failed before the change.
- A loader or an action gives the page of its request the status 403 with `Reply::status(403)` in PHP and `reply.status(403)` in Node (HY-69), so that an application shows a sign-in form in place of a page that needs a session, at the URL of that page. The page renders as it does with 200, as JSON or as a document, with `Cache-Control: no-store` and without `ETag`; a page with 409 or 422, a redirect and a stop keep their own status, and another status fails with 500. A loader could only stop the request with the plain text 403 of HY-51 or render the page with 200, which states that the response is the page. `AppTest` and `app.test.ts` failed before the change.
- The application can declare a disconnect hook, `App::open(onDisconnect:)` in PHP and the `onDisconnect` option of `App.open` in Node, which the server calls once with the request, the elapsed milliseconds and the reply of a request whose client closed the connection (HY-67). The Node server learns of the close from `node:http` and then runs no further loader, action or rendering of the request, writes no response and does not call the response hook. PHP reports a closed connection only when a write to it fails, so `App::run` turns `ignore_user_abort` off, PHP ends the script at the first failed write of the response, and a shutdown function calls the hook; the PHP server renders the complete response before it can detect the close. The servers ran every loader and rendered the response for a client that had gone, and reported nothing. `RunTest` and `http.test.ts` failed before the change.
- Every response with a status of 400 or more has `Cache-Control: no-store` (HY-65): the plain text responses that hyper answers itself, the 400 and 431 responses of the Node server to requests that `node:http` cannot parse, and the pages with status 409 or 422. The plain text responses had no `Cache-Control`, so a cache could store a 404 or 405 response by its heuristic freshness (RFC 9111). `AppTest`, `ClientTest` and the Node tests `app.test.ts`, `http.test.ts` and `client.test.ts` failed before the change; the 12 failure cases of `conformance/client.json` now state the header.
- The application declares a response limit, the largest response body in bytes (8 MiB by default): `App::open(responseLimit:)` in PHP and the `responseLimit` option of `App.open` in Node (HY-66). A response whose body is larger is not sent: the server logs the method, the path, the size and the limit, answers a plain 500 that no cache stores and reports it to the response hook. A limit that is not a positive integer fails when the application opens. The servers sent a body of any size, which a slow client keeps in the memory of the server process and of a web server that buffers the response. `AppTest` and `app.test.ts` failed before the change.
- `App::open` of PHP names the missing file of a server program: `program.php`, `program.json` or the directory `templates` (HY-48). It named only the program directory. `RendererTest` failed before the change.
- The browser applies the stylesheet links of the head when it shows new content (HY-64): of the rendered document in client-side rendering, of the layout rendered with the shared data of a region response, and of the HTML document of a history restoration in server-side rendering. It keeps a present link whose key, the `href` resolved against the page URL, the source has, inserts the missing links in the order of the source, waits until they load and only then shows the content, and afterwards removes the links that the source does not have. Client-side rendering replaced only the `html` attributes, the title and the body, and region navigation replaced only regions, so a client-rendered page applied no linked stylesheet, and a navigation to a page whose layout links another stylesheet did not apply it. A link that fails to load keeps the page and marks the region, or the body, with `hy-error="0"` (HY-47). The board post page now links `reader.css`, which `make assets` also copies into `dist/csr/assets/`. Seven cases of `tests/hyper.test.ts` and `tests/stylesheets.test.ts` failed before the change, and `make e2e` failed in SSR without `/assets/reader.css` after a navigation to a post and in CSR without any stylesheet link; the SSR and CSR flows now check the links after navigation, history restoration and a first document, the style of the post body when it first appears while its stylesheet response is delayed, and a stylesheet that fails to load. The bundle limits rise to 32,700 gzip bytes for the SSR script and 34,000 for the CSR shell, because this code adds 761 and 726 gzip bytes.
- The selection of the client rendering in the Node server may return a promise of `true` or `false`, which the server awaits (HY-62). A selection that reads the service of a host from a database reads it asynchronously in Node, and the server called the selection without waiting, so such a selection answered every request with 500. A promise that rejects or resolves to another value fails with HY-43. `tests/client.test.ts` failed before the change with status 500 for a selection that returns a promise.
- One server answers the client-rendered pages and the server-rendered pages of one manifest with the same handlers (HY-62). The application declares client rendering with `App::open(clientRendering: new ClientRendering(shell, basePath, selects))` in PHP and the `clientRendering` option of `App.open` in Node: the absolute path of the static shell, a data base path such as `/_props`, and a selection that chooses the requests of client-rendered pages, for example by the `Host` header. A chosen HTML `GET` of a page receives the shell with `Cache-Control: no-cache` and `Vary: Accept`, without a session or a loader; its JSON requests and actions run under the data base path and redirect within it; an HTML request under the data base path and a JSON request outside it receive 406. Other requests are answered with the base path of the application. The shell response carries the frame policy (HY-45) and is reported to the response hook (HY-60). Opening fails when the shell does not declare the data base path or a route lies under it. The servers bound every handler to one base path, so one process could not answer both forms, and the Node server could not answer with the shell. Both servers pass the 24 cases of `conformance/client.json`, which failed before the change.
- Client-side rendering gives the `html` element the attributes of the `html` element of the rendered layout, so a page whose data is in English has `lang="en"` (HY-63). The browser replaced only the title and the body, so the `lang="ko"` of the static shell stayed on every page; the shell that `scripts/build-assets.mjs` writes now has no `lang`. `tests/document.test.ts` failed before the change, and `make e2e` checks that the client-rendered board has the `html` element of the server-rendered board.
- `@polyspec/hyper` and `@polyspec/hyper-server` publish JavaScript modules with type declarations, which `npm run build` of each package (`make packages`) writes to `dist`; their `exports` name only `dist/index.js` and `dist/index.d.ts` (HY-61). The packages exported TypeScript source, which Node 26 does not run from `node_modules` (`ERR_UNSUPPORTED_NODE_MODULES_TYPE_STRIPPING`). Their source used parameter properties, which a type check with `erasableSyntaxOnly` rejects; the classes now declare their fields, and both packages and the board Node server type-check with `erasableSyntaxOnly`. `make package-check`, part of `make check`, installs both packages into `tests/package-install` with `npm install --install-links`, type-checks its test against the declarations and runs it under `node`; it failed with `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_NODE_MODULES_TYPE_STRIPPING` before the change. The Node server tests, the asset and server builds and the fixture builds now import the built packages.
- The response hook also receives the reply of the request, and a loader or an action records a value of the request for the hook with `Reply::note(name, value)` (`reply.note(name, value)` in Node), which the hook reads with `notes()` (HY-60). The Node server gave the hook the request as received and its loaders and actions a copy, so the hook could not read a value that they decided, such as the kind of a refusal; a PHP application used the state of its process. The reply is the one that the loaders and actions received, also after a stop or an error, and an empty reply when the server answered before routing. The notes are not part of the response. The tests of both servers failed before the change.
- The application can declare a response hook, `App::open(onResponse:)` in PHP and the `onResponse` option of `App.open` in Node, which the server calls once for every response with the request, the response and the elapsed milliseconds, including the 400, 403, 404, 405, 413, 415 and 500 responses that hyper answers itself (HY-60). An application logs refusals through it. `App::run` counts from `REQUEST_TIME_FLOAT`; the Node server counts from the time when `node:http` gives the request, and also reports the 400 and 431 responses to requests that `node:http` cannot parse.
- The application declares a body limit (8 MiB by default) and the form types that its actions and `/_hyper/keep` accept (`application/x-www-form-urlencoded` by default, and `multipart/form-data`): `App::open(bodyLimit:, formTypes:)` in PHP and the `bodyLimit` and `formTypes` options of `App.open` in Node (HY-59). Both servers answer 413 for a larger body, then 415 for another media type, then 403 for a wrong CSRF token, before any loader or action runs. The Node server option `bodyLimit` of `app.server` is removed; the server reads no more of a body than the limit of the application. PHP takes the size from `Content-Length` as well, because it leaves a body larger than `post_max_size` empty, and `App::run` fails when `post_max_size` is smaller than the limit or when multipart bodies are accepted while `enable_post_data_reading` is on. `make server-parity` now compares the 413, 415 and 403 responses of both servers: 49 responses were equal.
- A loader or an action can stop a request with `BadRequest`, which answers 400 with the text `Bad Request`, and an action can return `Result::page(status, data)` (`Result.page` in Node), which renders the route page with the status 200, 409 or 422 (HY-58). `Result::invalid(data)` is the page with 422. Only a `GET` request now receives 304 for a matching `If-None-Match`, so an action that renders a page with 200 always runs (HY-53).
- `Request` provides every form value of the body in order without nesting, as the query values are provided: `roles[]` stays the name `roles[]` with all its values (HY-57). PHP read `$_POST`, which nests bracketed names, and the Node server kept only the last value of a name; both servers now read the raw body, an `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body or the text fields of a `multipart/form-data` body. PHP reads a multipart body only when `enable_post_data_reading` is off. `formString` reads the last value of a name. Both servers pass the 4 multipart cases of `conformance/fields.json`.
- `Request` provides every query value in order without nesting, as an ordered map from each name to its values, and the raw query of the request target (HY-56). PHP read `$_GET`, which nests `a[]` and `a[b]` and keeps one value of a repeated name, so both servers now parse the raw query themselves. `queryInt` reads the last value of a name. Both servers pass the 17 cases of `conformance/fields.json`.
- `make server-parity`, part of `make check`, sends every request of the parity steps to the PHP server and the board Node server and requires the same status, headers and body after it replaces session identifiers, CSRF tokens and `ETag` values with placeholders (HY-55). It compared 41 responses equal. `BOARD_TIME` fixes the creation time of posts in both board servers.
- The Node.js server package `@polyspec/hyper-server` (`packages/hyper-node`) implements the rules of the PHP server: routes with a base path, loaders, actions with CSRF, results and stops, reply cookies and cache control, JSON tags, flash values and changed topics, kept values, request checks, plain errors, the data model check and sessions in files. It renders documents with the browser code and the template files of the asset build, writes JSON with the bytes of PHP `json_encode`, and decodes kept values as PHP `json_decode` does (HY-54). Loaders and actions receive one context with the request, the reply and the services. Its tests run the cases of the PHP server tests against the same fixtures, and `conformance/json.json` holds JSON cases that both servers pass, including U+2028 and U+2029, floats and empty maps. `make node-server` builds a Node server of the board example with `node:sqlite`, which renders the same documents as PHP.
- The request path must consist of printable ASCII characters, as RFC 9112 requires of a request target; both servers answer another path with 400 (HY-42). `node:http` rejects such a target before an application sees it, so the PHP server now follows the same rule.
- The manifest check of the browser code also requires a layout and a title template, and a title and a template for every route and route region, as the PHP manifest reader does.
- A route path can end with a rest parameter `{name*}`, which matches zero or more remaining request segments and holds the rest of the request path without decoding (HY-49). Both routers pass the cases of `conformance/rest.json`.
- A loader or an action can stop a request with `Redirect`, which answers 303 with the location, flash values and changed topics of a redirect result (HY-50), or with `Forbidden`, which answers 403 (HY-51).
- Loaders and actions receive the `Reply` of the request, which adds and removes cookies with the attributes of HY-45 and sets the `Cache-Control` of a page response; the response contains its cookies whatever its status (HY-52). A JSON page response has a strong `ETag`, and a request with the same `If-None-Match` receives 304 (HY-53). `Set-Cookie` is the one response header that holds several values.

### Fixed

- The timeout cases of `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs` assert behaviour, not wall time (H14.20, HY-89). They asserted that a run with a hanging test ended within 20 seconds, which a loaded machine can exceed while the runner works. Each case now awaits the end of the run, whose hanging test never ends by itself, and checks its output; the timeout of the case fails a runner that does not stop the test. A new case fails for an assertion on `Date.now()` or `performance.now()` differences in the tests of the check scripts.
- The output copies give their files the mode 0644 and their directories 0755 under any umask (H14.19, HY-68). They created them with those modes, which the umask of the process reduces, so under the umask 077 the outputs had the modes 0600 and 0700 and another user could not read them. The cause is now tested on every platform, under the umask 077 and with reads as `nobody` on the GitHub runner; the Apple `container` cases, which failed in a CI run with `spawnSync container ENOENT` because Linux has no `container`, run in `make virtiofs-check`, a target of the full suite on Darwin only.
- `tests/scripts/publish.test.mjs` runs on Linux (H14.18). Its file case gave a content of 200 000 bytes to its writer and reader processes as one argument, which Linux refuses above 131 072 bytes with `spawn E2BIG`, so `make test-scripts` failed in a CI run. The contents go through a file, and every argument of a child process of the test is checked against that limit.
- `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` passes under GNU Make 4 (H14.17, HY-83). It asserted the line `*** [fails] Error 3` of GNU Make 3.81, which GNU Make 4.4.1 of the CI runner writes as `*** [Makefile:28: fails] Error 3`, so `make test-scripts` failed in a CI run. The case asserts the last output of the recipe on both streams instead.
- The first failure lines of a CI target are the lines that a test reporter marks with `✖` and their details (H14.16, HY-91). In a CI run the summary of `test-scripts` began with `FAIL start ssr`, the output of a passing test that starts a failing server, and with the start lines `▶` of tests whose names hold the words error and failure, and showed the cause of only one of the four failed tests.
- The Rust toolchain and the crates of the native extension are installed only where the extension is built (H14.15, HY-89, HY-91). `make install` installed them for every checkout and CI job, also for the CI groups `docs` and `node`, which build no extension; the template repository had the same defect with VS Code (T20.1-6). `make install-rust` installs them, `make rust-downloads-check` names it, and only the CI groups `php` and `board` run it. The make install of the template branch still installs its Rust toolchain in the group `node`, because that branch has no target that installs its npm dependencies alone.
- Every step of a GitHub workflow runs one make target on the declared runner (H14.13, HY-90). The job `push-gate` ran `node scripts/push-gate.mjs commit <sha>` directly on `ubuntu-latest` with actions pinned by a major tag, so the settings and the checks of the Makefile did not apply to it and its runner and actions changed with their releases. It runs `make push-gate-commit COMMIT=<sha>` on `ubuntu-26.04-arm` with actions pinned by commit, and its steps after the first run with `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`. The new `tests/scripts/ci-workflow.test.mjs` fails for a step that runs a command other than one make, a job on another runner, an action without a commit pin, `timeout-minutes` and a later step without `!cancelled()`; it named 6 such problems of `push-gate.yml` before the change.
- Every check runs cargo, npm and Composer offline, and only `make tools` and `make install` download (H14.12, HY-89). `cargo build --locked` of `make ext` reached crates.io for a crate that no earlier command had downloaded, and `make template` ran `rustup toolchain install`, which reads the channel manifest, whenever `config/template.json` changed. The Makefile exports `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE`, `npm_config_offline` and `COMPOSER_DISABLE_NETWORK`, the downloads of `make tools` and `make install` lift them with `$(ONLINE)`, `make install` downloads the crates of the native extension with `cargo fetch --locked`, and `make rust-downloads-check`, which `make ext` runs first, fails for a missing toolchain or crate with the first error line of cargo and `run make install`. `tests/scripts/toolchain.test.mjs` and `tests/scripts/rust-downloads.test.mjs` failed in 5 cases before the change.
- Every line of a check log starts at column 0 (H14.11, HY-84). Pint prints its result without a final newline, so the next command of `lint` and the line `[full-run] lint passed` joined that line in the full run of 2026-10-05, and a line-based reader such as the `lastLines` of a failed target would join two lines. The checks of a recipe run through `scripts/line-end.mjs`, and the full run ends the output of make the same way.
- PHP is pinned by its minor release 8.5 and make is not pinned (H14.2-2, HY-81). H14.2 pinned PHP 8.5.10 and make 3.81 exactly, so a PHP patch release or a GNU Make 4 machine would have failed every recipe, although the template repository pins PHP by its minor and showed that the make release does not change the expansion of its targets. `toolchain-check` compares the PHP minor, and the full-run record names the running releases of node, npm, PHP, Composer and make as `environment`. The pin cases of `tests/scripts/toolchain.test.mjs` and the record case of `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` failed with the earlier pins.
- No result depends on a live service, on the timing of a step or on a port that a run does not own (H14.10, HY-89). `npm ci` queried the audit service on every install, `tests/package-install` of `make package-check` resolved `htmx.org` from the registry without a lock, the disconnect case of `http.test.ts` raced timers of 50, 300 and 1500 ms, `RunTest` chose a free port and started its server on it later, a server start without its ready line waited without a word, and an empty template index failed `check-bundle-size.mjs` with a TypeError. Installs run without an audit, `tests/package-install` installs offline from its tracked `package-lock.json`, the disconnect case waits for the close that the server has seen, the PHP server of `RunTest` starts on port 0, a waiting server start prints its progress every 5 seconds, and the bundle check names the empty list.
- Every tracked path has owners, and `make owner-check` runs the owners of the changed paths (H14.9, HY-88). AGENTS asked for the tests that own a change, and nothing declared them: several scripts had no test that named them, and H14.3 changed `scripts/full-run.mjs` without running `tests/scripts/push-gate.test.mjs`, which then failed on main. `scripts/owner-checks.json` declares the owners, `scripts/owner-check.mjs` validates the declaration and runs the owners of the changed paths, `tests/scripts/ignored-files.test.mjs` checks `.gitignore`, and AGENTS requires `make owner-check` before a commit.
- `tests/scripts/push-gate.test.mjs` copies the import closure of the push gate into its temporary checkouts (H14.3-1). H14.3 made `scripts/full-run.mjs` import `scripts/holder-lock.mjs`, the test copied a fixed list of scripts, and its 5 cases that run the gate failed with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`; H14.3 was committed without this test because no owner map selected it, which the owner check of H14.9 found.
- No server or temporary directory of a run outlives it (H14.8, HY-87). `startBoard` did not await the stop of the servers that had started when another failed, a check that ended on a signal left its PHP server and its directory, and `serve-demo.test.mjs` removed the directory of a running demo before it stopped it, because node:test runs the hooks of a test in the order of registration. `serverRun` of `scripts/board-servers.mjs` owns the directory and the children of a run and closes them on the end, the failure, SIGINT and SIGTERM; the tests stop their processes before they remove their directories, and `RunTest` releases its server and log in `finally`. The new cases of `tests/scripts/board-servers.test.mjs` and `tests/scripts/check-parity.test.mjs` failed before.
- A recipe with several checks runs every check to its end and names each failed check (H14.7, HY-86). A failed recipe line ended the recipe, so a failed vitest run hid the type check, a failed Pint run hid the other, and a failed parity with the generated program hid the one with the native extension. `template-check`, `test-js`, `test-node`, `test-php`, `lint`, `parity` and `bench-server` run their checks with `$(call check,<name>,<command>)` and end with `$(checks_result)`. The 3 cases of `tests/scripts/check-recipes.test.mjs` failed with the earlier Makefile.
- Tests create what they read or name it as the output of another target (H14.6, HY-85). `rejects an invalid manifest and writes nothing` read the tracked fixture directory, so an `out` directory of an earlier run failed it, and `template-dir.test.mjs` copied the npm copy of the template package and failed with `ENOENT` without it. The asset build tests copy the tracked files of their fixtures into temporary directories, `template-dir.test.mjs` reads the declared template copy of `TEMPLATE_DIR`, and `requireBuilt` of `tests/scripts/requires.mjs` names the missing input and the make target that writes it in the tests that read installs or builds of other targets.
- A check passes only when it checked something, and the full run states the cause of each failed target (H14.5, HY-84). An empty directory, a name pattern or a filter that selected nothing passed with 0 tests in node, vitest and PHPUnit, a node test registered after a top-level await was dropped by `--test-force-exit` without a count, `check-parity.mjs` passed a request file without a checked step, and the summary of the full run named only the failed targets. `scripts/run-tests.mjs` fails a run in which no test ran and names the expected and the actual count, `phpunit.xml` fails an empty suite, a case requires that no node test file awaits at its top level, `check-parity.mjs` fails without a status or compare step and without a compared Node response, and the full run records and prints the last 20 lines of each failed target. The new cases of `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs`, `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` and `tests/scripts/check-parity.test.mjs` failed before.
- Checks read tool output in a form that does not change with the run context or an unpinned release (H14.4, HY-83). `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` asserted the lines of `make -n`, which a make inside another make or with `-w` prints between `make: Entering directory` lines, so it failed with `MAKEFLAGS=w`. `dryRun` of `tests/scripts/make-dry-run.mjs` starts make without the variables of a calling make and with `--no-print-directory`, and the three test files use it. The Rust check of the template copy reads `release:` of `rustc -vV` instead of the text of the unpinned rustup.
- Outputs that other processes read are published without a missing or partly written file (H14.3, HY-82). The copies, the server program and the static deployment were removed and then written, the npm and Composer copies were removed and installed again, single files were written in place, the copies shared the staging name `<output>.next`, and two full runs could both decide to run: a reader of the copy of `packages/hyper-php` found a file missing in 1029 of 16904 reads while it was written 5 times. `scripts/publish.mjs` moves each file of a staging directory of the writing process into the target with one rename and then removes stale files; `writeFileAtomic` writes single files with one rename; an installed copy is published only while its dependency tree stays the same and fails otherwise with the install command. The asset build takes the files of the static deployment from `--static`. The package manager installs hold `var/install.lock` and the full run `var/full-run.lock`. The 5 cases of `tests/scripts/publish.test.mjs` pass.
- Every recipe and script starts npm and Composer by the absolute paths of `var/tools/bin` (H14.2-1, HY-81). make 3.81 looks up the program of a recipe line without shell syntax on the `PATH` of its own process, so the simple `npm` and `composer` lines of H14.2 ran the tools of the machine although the Makefile exported `var/tools/bin` first on `PATH`. `tests/scripts/toolchain.test.mjs` reads the commands of `make -n` of 14 targets and requires the absolute paths; it failed with the Makefile of H14.2.
- Every tool is an exact release pinned in a tracked file, and npm and Composer are installed into the checkout (H14.2, HY-81). `make ext` ran cargo in the root of this checkout, where rustup chose the default toolchain 1.98.0 instead of the 1.98.1 of the template repository, and npm, Composer, PHP and make were whatever the machine had. `package.json` records npm 12.2.0 with the SHA-512 of its tarball in `packageManager`, `config/toolchain.json` records PHP 8.5.10, Composer 2.10.3 with the SHA-256 of its phar and make 3.81, and the template copy holds `rust-toolchain.toml`. `make tools` installs npm and Composer into `var/tools`, never into the machine, whose tools every checkout and session shares; `var/tools/bin` is first on `PATH` for every recipe and every tool of `scripts/run-tests.mjs`. `make toolchain-check` runs before every recipe that runs npm, Composer, PHP or cargo and names the expected and the actual value of each tool that differs. `make ext` builds in the extension directory of the copy with `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` and built the extension with Rust 1.98.1. `make docs-check` checks the Markdown files that Git tracks; it walked the checkout and failed in the documentation of the npm of `var/tools`. The 6 cases of `tests/scripts/toolchain.test.mjs` pass; they failed with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before.
- The declared template copy is the commit at the head of the branch that the tracked `config/template.json` names (H14.1, HY-80). Every target of the full run copied the working tree of `../template` again, so the run of 2026-10-05 failed in 7 targets that read a `dist` the template repository was rebuilding, and the copy parsed `npm pack --json`, which npm 12.2.0 prints as an object, so `make template` failed with `TypeError: object is not iterable`. The copy now takes the commit at the head of that branch whatever commit the template checkout is at, and fails with the expected and the actual value for a missing branch, inputs of the TypeScript build that are not the files of the commit, a build record of other inputs and a record or build that changes while npm packs; it takes the tracked paths from the head commit with `git archive`, takes the packed file from an empty directory and writes `var/products/template/copy.json`. `make template` copies only when `config/template.json` or the copy script changed, and the full-run record names the branch. The 8 cases of `tests/scripts/template-copy.test.mjs` and the 11 cases of `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` pass; 5 copy cases failed with the script of main and the full-run case failed without the `template` field.
- The execution checklist holds only headings and task tables, and `make docs-check` fails for any other line with its file, line and column (H7.1-1-2). The checklist held a translation link, a paragraph on its content, the How to use rules and a paragraph of dependencies and background under each wave, and no check told such text from tasks. The rules are in AGENTS, and the dependencies and the background of the waves are in `docs/plans/waves.md`, which each wave heading links. The case of `tests/scripts/check-documents.test.mjs` failed before the change.
- `make docs-check` also fails for an x or a capital X between brackets in the execution checklist that is not the state of a task row, because a Markdown reader takes these forms as task list states (H7.1-1-1). The case of `tests/scripts/check-documents.test.mjs` failed before the change, because the check reported neither form.
- A task state marker appears in the execution checklist only as the state of a task row, at the start of its last cell (H7.1-1). The legend of the checklist and the texts of wave 7, H7.1, H11.1 and H11.1-1 wrote markers in inline code, so a tool that counted markers counted tasks in progress that do not exist. `make docs-check` now fails for any other marker in both languages and names its file, line and column; the legend is removed, AGENTS defines the states, and the texts name states in words. `tests/scripts/check-documents.test.mjs` failed before the change, because the check printed no problem for a fixture with markers in a legend, a task text, a bypass cause and a row that is not a task.
- Each npm package declares a build that runs the TypeScript compiler by the path of its package (H12.6-1). H12.6 removed the bin links, and a build of `packages/hyper-js` with `npm exec --offline --no -- tsc` failed with `This is not the tsc command you are looking for`, so H12.6 was reverted. `scripts/tsc.mjs` resolves `typescript/package.json` and runs its `bin.tsc` with the arguments of the call; `npm run build` (`tsconfig.build.json`) and `npm run tsc -- <arguments>` of `packages/hyper-js` and `packages/hyper-node` and `TSC` of the Makefile use it. `tests/scripts/package-build.test.mjs` builds each package into a temporary directory with `npm run build -- --outDir <directory>` and with `npm run tsc -- -p tsconfig.build.json --outDir <directory>`: the cases failed without the declared builds and pass with them, while `npm exec --offline --no -- tsc` fails in the copy install.
- `make analyse-php` runs PHPStan with `--memory-limit=256M` (H12.5). Without its result cache `build/phpstan`, as in a new worktree, the PHPStan worker needs 132 MB and crashed with `PHPStan process crashed because it reached configured PHP memory limit: 128M`, so `make check` failed in `analyse-php`; with the cache it needed less and passed. After `rm -rf build/phpstan`, `make analyse-php` failed twice before the change and passes after it.
- `make serve-demo` runs once on a machine, and `make bench-browser` uses servers and a database of its own run (H12.4). Two demos started PHP and the edge on the same fixed ports 8080 to 8082 with their output discarded, so the second demo failed silently or served beside the first. The demo keeps its fixed ports and now holds the lock file `/tmp/hyper-serve-demo.lock` of the new `scripts/holder-lock.mjs`, which is created atomically with its whole content and names the checkout, the process ID and the start time of the holder: a second demo fails with that holder before it starts a server, the demo stops its servers and releases the lock on SIGINT and SIGTERM, and a lock of an ended demo is reported and stays until `make serve-demo-unlock` removes it, which fails while the demo runs. The demo starts its servers with `scripts/board-servers.mjs` and prints their output. `bench-browser` used the fixed ports 8085 to 8087, `examples/board/var/bench-browser.db` and a polling start check; it now starts its servers with `scripts/board-servers.mjs` on ports that the system assigns and its database in a temporary directory of the run, and its options `--ssr`, `--edge` and `--api` are removed. `tests/scripts/serve-demo.test.mjs` failed in all three cases before the change and passes after it; `node scripts/bench-browser.mjs --runs 1` ran in 21 s while another server held 127.0.0.1:8086 and removed its run directory.
- `make e2e` runs the browser tests on servers of its own run (H12.3). Playwright started `scripts/serve-demo.mjs` through `webServer` on the fixed ports 8090, 8091 and 8093 with the database `examples/board/var/e2e.db`, which the Makefile removed before every run, so a run from another checkout or session failed to listen or reset the database of a running run. The new `scripts/run-e2e.mjs` starts the API origin, the edge and the SSR origin with `scripts/board-servers.mjs` on ports that the system assigns and an empty database in a temporary directory of the run, without a time limit for the start, runs `playwright test` with the addresses in `HYPER_E2E_SSR` and `HYPER_E2E_CSR`, which `tests/e2e/origins.ts` requires, and then stops the servers and removes the directory; each case keeps its Playwright timeout. `tests/scripts/run-e2e.test.mjs` lists the cases through the runner while another server holds 127.0.0.1:8091 and runs Playwright without the runner; both cases failed before the change and pass after it.
- `make parity` and `make server-parity` test the servers of their own run (H12.2). `scripts/check-parity.mjs` started PHP on the fixed port 8092 or 8094 and the board Node server on 8096 with their output discarded, accepted any server that answered on that port, and used the fixed databases `examples/board/var/parity.db` and `parity-node.db` and the session directory `parity-sessions`: while another server answered on 127.0.0.1:8092 with 500, a status step of the old check failed with `statuses 500 and 500, expected 404`, because PHP could not listen and the check tested the other server, and two runs reset each other's database. The new `scripts/board-servers.mjs` starts each server on a port that the system assigns, prints its output with the prefix `[<name>]`, and takes the address from the line in which the server reports that it listens, without a time limit and failing when the server exits first; the board Node server and `scripts/serve-edge.mjs` print the port that they listen on. The databases and the session directory lie in a temporary directory of the run. The options `--port` and `--node-port` (now `--node`) and the polling `waitForServer` are removed. `tests/scripts/check-parity.test.mjs` runs a fixture PHP application while another server holds 127.0.0.1:8092; both cases failed before the change and pass after it.
- `tests/package-install` expects the export `./templates-index` of `@polyspec/hyper` with its declaration file (H10.3-1). H10.3 added the export, and the package install test still required the export `.` alone, so `make package-check` failed with a difference in `exports`.
- Made the server build check the templates with `templateProblems` (HC-6, HY-3, HY-30, HY-75) before it removes or writes any output (HY-48, H10.2). Only `make templates-check` ran these rules, on the board example, so a server program built with `build-server.mjs` served templates that broke them. The first attempt (H10.2) also failed the build of a manifest that declares a route region that no template places, and was reverted. A script test builds the region fixture and requires the build to fail with the problem of the lost region and to leave no output; it failed before the change.
- Made the template rules of `make templates-check` see the placements and includes inside the branches of a condition (HY-3, HY-30, HY-75, H10.2-1). The walk visited only lists whose items are nodes, and the branches of `{? }` are objects without a type, so a region placed in a branch, or in a template that a branch includes, counted 0 times. The rules moved into `templateProblems` of `scripts/template-rules.mjs`, so that the server build can use them (H10.2). The region fixture adds a route with a region placed in a branch and one placed through an include in a branch; the check reported both as missing before the change.
- Made the PHP server reject an application object in the values of the shared handler and the loaders with HY-43, as the Node server does (HY-44, H10.1). Host binding keeps an application object such as `DateTimeImmutable` as a native object (VAL-19), and the PHP check only bound the values, so PHP answered 200 where Node answered 500. `DataModel::check` binds a value and fails on a native object; the document check, the JSON response check and the kept value check use it, and `Kept::inDataModel` became `DataModel::contains`. A PHP `stdClass` and a `JsonSerializable` still pass, because binding turns them into values of the data model. The new PHP case failed before the change; the Node case pins the existing behavior.
- The read paths of a template end when a loop variable rebinds a name that an assignment before the loop read, as in `{:p = page.x}{@ page = p.items}{= page.title}{/}` (H2.6, HY-73). The analysis stored the loop variable as a local variable of the whole template, so the assignment read the loop variable, the loop source grew by `items.*` in every round, and the rounds never ended: `scripts/build-server.mjs` ran at full CPU without end on such a template. The loop variable now exists only in the body of its loop, where it has the paths of its source followed by `*`. `conformance/reads.json` holds the two cases `{:p = page.x}{@ page = p.items}{= page.title}{/}` and `{:p = q.x}{@ q = p.items}{/}`, and `reads.test.ts` computes the read paths of every case in a separate process with a limit of 10 seconds, so an analysis that does not end fails its case; before the change both cases failed at that limit.
- The PHP server fails a request whose shared handler or loader returns a value that is not a map, such as a list, with the plain 500 of HY-43 (HY-44). PHP merged a list into the shared data or used it as region data and answered 200, while the Node server already failed the request. Before the change `AppTest::testSharedDataAndRegionDataThatAreNotMapsFail` received 200 for a shared handler that returned `["b"]`; the Node server test of the same cases passes before and after the change.
- The PHP manifest check rejects `regions` or `routes` that are not lists and a region name or a route name that is not a string (HY-2). It accepted `"routes": "home"` with a PHP warning and no routes, the route name `5` as `"5"`, and the region name `[]` as `"Array"` with a warning. Before the change `AppTest::testManifestRejectsListsAndNamesOfAnotherType` accepted each of its three fixtures.
- The PHP server counts the elapsed time of the response hook and the disconnect hook on the monotonic clock (HY-60): `App::run(?int $started)` and `handle(..., ?int $started)` take an `hrtime(true)` start from their caller, or count from their own call. `App::run` subtracted `REQUEST_TIME_FLOAT` from `microtime(true)`, two wall clock times, so a system time that moved backwards during a request gave a negative elapsed time; a request log showed `-1.419ms`. `RunTest` failed before the change with `-9998.7` ms for a `REQUEST_TIME_FLOAT` ten seconds after now, and `AppTest` and `RunTest` failed for an `hrtime(true)` start.
- The asset build and the server build copy their output files with the mode 0644 and their directories with the mode 0755 (HY-68). They copied with `fs.cpSync` of Node, which creates its destination files with the mode 0200, and a virtiofs bind mount of a Linux container refuses that creation, so `scripts/build-server.mjs` failed in a container with `EACCES, Permission denied` for its `templates` directory. `tests/scripts/output-files.test.mjs` runs the server build and the copies in the official Node image with the output on a virtiofs bind mount of Apple `container`; it failed before the change.
- The build scripts read the browser package from the working directory: `data-template.json` from `packages/hyper-js/` and `checkManifest` and `templateReferences` from the build output in `packages/hyper-js/dist`. A build therefore failed outside the repository directory and without `dist`, and a build had to write `dist` into this repository first. The scripts now read `data-template.json` and the source of `packages/hyper-js` next to them; `make server`, `make server-fixtures` and `make node-fixtures` no longer run `make packages`. `tests/scripts/package-source.test.mjs` failed before the change with `./dist/index.js` not found and with `ENOENT` for `packages/hyper-js/data-template.json`.
- The PHP benchmark failed since `Polyspec\Hyper\Examples\Board\Posts` took a clock, because the benchmark is not part of `make check`. It now passes a fixed clock, and `make check` runs `make bench-server-smoke`, which runs the PHP benchmark once per measurement. The browser benchmark takes several minutes and stays outside `make check`; `docs/operations/benchmark.md` states this. The benchmark document now holds the results of the current code (2026-10-02, measured on a machine with load average 8): for 1,000 rows the generated PHP program renders in 12.8 ms and the native extension in 3.6 ms.
- The bundle limits rise to 32,500 gzip bytes for the SSR script and 33,800 for the CSR shell. The template runtime, which checks every number of the data model, grows the SSR script to 32,057 and the CSR shell to 33,364 gzip bytes; the hyper code adds 79 of them.
- The PHP session added `Expires`, `Pragma` and a second `Cache-Control` header to every response of a session, so a JSON response had two `Cache-Control` headers and a reply could not make a page cacheable. PHP also gave responses without a body, such as 303 and 204, the type `text/html`. The session now adds only its cookie, every page response has `Cache-Control: no-store` unless the reply sets it for status 200, and `App::run` turns off the default content type (HY-52). A comparison of complete response headers found these defects; `AppTest`, `RequestTest` and `RunTest` failed before the fix.
- These defects of swaps, kept values, redirects and sessions are fixed with tests that failed before:
  - The fix of the call order (HY-33) ended a `set`, `render` or `hy-set` change on any response, even one that did not contain its region, after the swap had already changed the page. The page then showed a value that was neither held nor stored. A change now ends only when the data of its own region was replaced, before the swap; when that happens during the swap, the region renders again from held data (HY-33).
  - The browser passed route regions to the page region as template definitions, so a route region saw the page region data in region responses but not in documents; after HY-48 the PHP document and an htmx navigation to the same URL could differ. Server and browser now both render route regions alone and pass HTML (HY-12, HY-13). `make parity` did not cover this case; a test now does.
  - Browser kept values were applied in declaration order instead of the order of HY-38 (browser storage, then pending server values).
  - Client-side rendering decoded a document against the requested path instead of the URL after redirects (HY-22).
  - Every request started a session and wrote a session file, including requests rejected before routing and paths without a route (HY-45).
The following defects of correctness and security are fixed. Each one has a test that failed before the fix.

- PHP server:
  - An unhandled exception printed the message, file paths and the stack trace, and one stored invalid UTF-8 byte broke the list for every visitor. The server now rejects input that is not UTF-8 with 400 (HY-42) and answers other failures with a plain 500 (HY-43).
  - `parse_url` read `/board/12:30` as `/` and `//board/1` as a host. The request path is now the request target before `?` or `#` (HY-42).
  - A POST with a non-page `Hy-Region` ran its action. The check now runs before loaders and actions (HY-16).
  - JSON carried integers outside the template data model with status 200. Both documents and JSON now fail with 500 (HY-44).
  - The session cookie had no `HttpOnly`, `SameSite` or `Secure`, and the server accepted identifiers that it did not create (HY-45).
  - Redirect locations such as `/\evil.example` were accepted (HY-46). `/_hyper/keep` stored values of any size; values longer than 4,096 bytes are now rejected (HY-40).
  - Empty kept maps and `stdClass` maps were not applied, and array spreading renumbered numeric data keys.
- Browser code:
  - After an SSR history restore, the browser kept the data of the earlier page; it now reads `#hy-data` of the restored document (HY-32).
  - Overlapping CSR restorations could mount the wrong page; only the latest one is rendered (HY-23).
  - CSR embedded browser kept values in `#hy-data`; the embedded data and JSON now stay server data, and browser values are applied only to held data. A `server` value whose saving has not completed now takes precedence over responses (HY-38, HY-39).
  - `set` added missing map keys (HY-33). A change through a parent or child of a kept path did not store it (HY-39). Region requests under a base path whose path already started with the base path were not prefixed (HY-23). `hy-set` rejected a string that ends with a backslash (HY-36).
  - A region request that received a non-JSON response swapped the body as HTML; it now does not swap and marks the region with `hy-error` (HY-47).
  - The client bundle contained the source of `hyper/data.tpl`; it now contains only the name (HY-34). Template references were computed by untested build code; `templateReferences` now lives in the package with tests.
- Example and development tools:
  - The comparison page ran `javascript:` URLs from `?ssr=` and accepted messages from any origin. Frames sent their whole body, including the CSRF token, to any parent. Frames now send a SHA-256 of the body to the embedding origin only, and the page accepts only http(s) origins.
  - The edge server cached `index.html` for a year; only files under `/assets/` are cached now.
- More defects of restorations, saves, request checks and failure marks are fixed with tests: a slow CSR restoration overwrote a later navigation (HY-23); a failed server save dropped its pending value, and a successful one could clear a later change (HY-39); one unrelated non-UTF-8 cookie blocked every page, so HY-42 now checks only `hy-keep` and the session cookie; `render`, `set` and `hy-set` changed held data before rendering and left failures unmarked (HY-47); kept values outside the data model caused lasting 500s and are now ignored or rejected (HY-38, HY-40); the request path of `?/x` and of absolute-form targets was wrong (HY-42); marks on regions outside the page region were not cleared, and cancelled requests were marked (HY-47); absolute and relative same-origin URLs were not prefixed (HY-23); redirect locations allowed C1 controls and invalid UTF-8 (HY-46); the session cookie could not be `Secure` behind a TLS-terminating CDN, and no response limited framing (HY-45). The example and the scripts start PHP with `display_errors=0`, because PHP prints startup warnings before the application runs (HY-43).
- More defects of the changes from "Keep region data in the server session, a cookie or Web Storage" to "Fix defects of kept data, requests and failures" are fixed with tests:
  - Two `set` calls at the same time lost one change. A change also stored the value of a response that arrived while it rendered, and swapped stale content over it. Calls now run in call order, and a change whose held data a response replaced ends without swapping, holding or storing (HY-33).
  - Saves of one path could arrive out of order, and an older failed save marked a region whose latest value was saved. Saves of a path are now sent one at a time with the latest value, and only the save of the latest change marks the region (HY-39).
  - A failure of `holdEmbedded` (template loading, rendering or unreadable embedded data) was not marked and rejected unhandled. It now marks the regions or the body and does not reject (HY-47).
  - A request that htmx aborted on its timeout was not marked. It is now a network failure (HY-47).
  - Kept maps and lists were checked only by their outer type, so a stored value with another inner shape caused 500 on every document. Kept values now conform to the shape of the data (HY-38). A conforming value that still breaks rendering makes its region render with its loader data (HY-38).
  - Query and form names nested in arrays skipped the UTF-8 check (HY-42).
  - Redirect locations accepted `.` and `..` segments, such as `/.//evil.example` (HY-46).
- `make templates-check` also checks that the layout places `{# title}`, `{# data}` and every manifest region once (HY-3), and manifests reject invalid topic names.

### Changed

- The declared template copy is the commit of the tag `TEMPLATE_TAG` (`v0.0.1`) of the template repository (H14.1-4, HY-80). The Makefile declares `TEMPLATE_TAG := v0.0.1` once; `scripts/copy-template.mjs --tag v0.0.1` resolves `refs/tags/v0.0.1^{commit}`, copies that commit whatever commit the branch `main` is at, records the tag and its commit in `var/products/template/copy.json`, and fails for a missing tag with the expected tag and the tags of the checkout. `make template-tag`, which `make template` runs before the copy, fails for a template checkout without the tag. `make check` and `make rerun-failed` pass `TEMPLATE_TAG` to `scripts/full-run.mjs`, which keys a run by the tree and the commit of the tag. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` checks out the template repository at `v0.0.1`, and `ci-workflow.test.mjs` requires that ref to equal `TEMPLATE_TAG`. Until version 0.1 a dependency on another polyspec repository is a GitHub tag of that repository. 8 of the 10 cases of `template-copy.test.mjs`, 10 of the 15 cases of `full-run.test.mjs` and the new case of `ci-workflow.test.mjs` failed before the change; the 33 cases pass after it.
- The build reads the template branch `main` as it is (H14.1-3, HY-78, HY-48). The declared copy holds the sources, the build configuration and the lock of the TypeScript package, whose digest the compiler of the template repository records, and the C sources, the stub and the build script of the native extension. `make ext` builds `build/ext/polyspec_template.so` with that script, phpize and the php-config of `PATH`, and PHPStan reads the stub `packages/template-php-ext/src/polyspec_template.stub.php`. `make server-fixtures`, `package-source.test.mjs` and `make analyse-php` failed against the branch `main` before the change and pass with it; `make test-php` passes with the generated program and with the extension.
- A full run is keyed by the tree and the commit of the template branch (H14.1-2, HY-80). `make check` and `make rerun-failed` pass `TEMPLATE_REPOSITORY` and `TEMPLATE_BRANCH` to `scripts/full-run.mjs`, which reads the commit of the branch when it starts and records it in the ignored `var/full-run.json`; a new commit of the branch permits a new full run of the same tree, and `make rerun-failed` reruns only targets of a run of the current tree and template commit. The 15 cases of `full-run.test.mjs` pass.
- The declared template copy is the commit at the head of the branch `main` of the template repository (H14.1-1, HY-80). `scripts/copy-template.mjs --branch main` resolves the branch when it runs and fails for a missing branch, and it writes nothing while `var/products/template/copy.json` records the same branch, commit, input hash and script hash. `make template` runs the copy every time and installs the template packages again only when `copy.json` is newer than `installed.stamp`. The CI workflow checks out the template repository at `main`, `make ci-pins` gives only the PHP minor, and the records of the full run and of a CI group name a run by its tree. The 9 cases of `template-copy.test.mjs`, the 15 cases of `full-run.test.mjs` and the 9 cases of `ci-run.test.mjs` pass.
- A new push stops the CI run of the same ref that is still going (H14.23, HY-91). The runners are few, and each push queued a full run behind the earlier ones. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` declares `concurrency` with the group `${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}` and `cancel-in-progress: true`; the push gate keeps every run.
- `make bundle-size` measures and never fails on a size (H14.22). It failed when a gzip size exceeded its limit in `config/bundle-size.json`, although performance is measured and reported. It prints each size with its limit and, for a size above its limit, a `WARNING` line, on GitHub an annotation `::warning::`, which the CI summary lists under warnings, and passes; a missing input still fails. A case of `tests/scripts/check-bundle-size.test.mjs` fails for a measuring script that ends with a failure.
- Development and commits run only the Red and Green unit tests of a change and `make docs-check`; no local `make owner-check`, `make check` or end-to-end check is required before a commit or a push, and CI runs the full suite after the push (H14.21). The pre-push hook stays the gate of tasks in progress and runs no test. `make virtiofs-check`, which only Darwin runs, therefore runs only in a local `make check` on a Mac.
