- No directory `node_modules` or `vendor` holds a symbolic link (H12.6, HY-79). npm linked the workspace packages `@polyspec/hyper` and `@polyspec/hyper-server` into `node_modules` and wrote bin links into `node_modules/.bin` and `tests/package-install/node_modules/.bin`, and Composer linked `polyspec/hyper` into the board vendor directory. `.npmrc` sets `install-links=true` and `bin-links=false`; the root package declares the two packages as `file:` dependencies with an override instead of npm workspaces, which npm always links; the packages have no npm scripts, `make packages` builds them with TypeScript and installs their copies again, `make package-check` installs without bin links, and the recipes and runners start TypeScript, esbuild, Vitest and Playwright with the files of their packages. Composer installs `polyspec/hyper` in the board from `var/products/hyper-php`, the copy of the tracked files of `packages/hyper-php` that `scripts/copy-package.mjs` writes (`make hyper-php-copy`, run by `make server`), because a mirror of `packages/hyper-php` also copied its `vendor` (83 MB). `scripts/tracked-files.mjs` holds the tracked-file copy that `scripts/copy-template.mjs` also uses. `tests/scripts/no-symlinks.test.mjs` fails for every symbolic link below a `node_modules` or `vendor` directory and passes on a checkout installed with the change.
- This repository reads the template repository only through its declared copy `var/products/template` (H12.1, HY-78). node_modules linked `@polyspec/template` to `../template/packages/template-ts`, whose build empties `dist` (tsup `clean: true`) and writes it again, `make template` ran that build in the template repository, and Composer, PHPStan and `make ext` read the template checkout, so a concurrent build of the template repository changed or broke a build or check of this repository while it ran. `scripts/copy-template.mjs` writes the copy from `TEMPLATE_REPOSITORY` (`../template`): the files that `npm pack --ignore-scripts` packs from the built TypeScript package, and the tracked files of `packages/template-php`, `packages/template-php-ext`, `packages/template-rust`, `tools/compiler` and `contracts/functions.json` with their modification times; it fails when the TypeScript package has no build and names `make build-ts` of the template repository. `make template` and `make install` write the copy; `make template` reinstalls the npm copy (`install-links=true`, a root dependency and an override) and the Composer copies (`reference: config`, version `dev-main`) from it, and `make template-check` also compares the npm copy. The Makefile names the copy as `TEMPLATE_DIR`, PHPStan reads its stubs, `make ext` builds from it, and the script tests read it from `TEMPLATE_DIR`. `tests/scripts/template-copy.test.mjs` failed in four of five cases before the change (npm installed `@polyspec/template` as a link into the template repository) and passes after it.
- The gzip limits of the SSR script and the CSR shell are measured again by the rule of the development guide, the measured size plus about 1 % rounded up to 100 bytes: 33,500 for 33,080 bytes and 33,600 for 33,224 bytes (H10.6). Since the measurement of 2026-10-04 the SSR script grew by 441 gzip bytes and the CSR shell by 442, so `make bundle-size` failed. The bundle took code that its renders call: the bound map runtime of template T14.2 (`bind`, `merge`, `BoundMap` and the bound map cases of `bindValue`, `bindMap` and `bindData`), 982 minified bytes; the root binding of H10.4 in `response.ts` (`bindParts`, `renderRegionOf`), 341 bytes; the rename of H10.5, 13 bytes. No module of the growth is unused or duplicated. The earlier SSR limit 32,700 did not follow the rule, which gave 33,000 for 32,639 bytes; this change corrects it.
- `scripts/check-parity.mjs` prints a line when a step starts and its result with the elapsed milliseconds, gives every request a timeout of 10 seconds with `AbortSignal.timeout`, and fails by the name of the step when a server does not answer (H11.3). It sent its requests without a timeout and printed a line only after a step ended, so a server that accepted a request and did not answer stopped `make parity` and `make server-parity` without naming the step. `tests/scripts/check-parity.test.mjs` runs the check against a server that never answers one step; before the change the check had not ended after 20 seconds. `make test-scripts` builds the npm packages first, because the check imports the browser package.
- `make test-js`, `make test-node`, `make test-php`, `make test-scripts` and `make package-check` run vitest, `node --test` and PHPUnit through `scripts/run-tests.mjs` and its progress reporters (H11.2). Every test prints its start, a line every 5 seconds while it runs and its result with the elapsed time, and has its own timeout of 30 seconds, at which it fails by its name. `node --test` and PHPUnit had no per-test timeout, so a test that never settled stopped the run without an end: a scratch test that waited one hour still ran after 40 seconds under `node --test` and under PHPUnit. vitest stopped it at its default 5 seconds but printed nothing until the file ended. `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs` runs such a test under each tool and requires the failure by name within 20 seconds. The runner counts only tests: PHPUnit also reports a class and a data provider method as suites, and the runner does not count them.
- The Required checks of AGENTS run only the tests that own a change before a commit, mark a task `[o]` with the command that owns it, and run `make check` exactly once, when every active task is done (H11.1). They asked for `make check` before a feature was marked implemented, and the task rows named `make check` as their verification, so the whole suite ran for each task. New task rows name their owning command; rows that are already `[o]` keep theirs.
- A document and a region response bind each root once with the bound map of the template engine and render every part with `merge` of the bound roots (H10.4, HY-13): the shared data, the data of every region and, in a document, the embedded data. Each render bound the shared data and its region data again, so a document checked the shared data once per render: the PHP test of a document without route regions counted 5 binds of a shared value and the JavaScript tests 4 to 6, against 2 and 1 now, with one more bind in the embedded data of a document with route regions. A JSON response checks only the request parameters again (HY-44), because the check before pruning covers the handler data. The PHP `Renderer` takes bound maps and binds with the bound map of its program, the native extension or the generated program; hyper-js has `bindParts` and `renderRegionOf`. With hyper H10.4 the hyper PHP tests pass with the generated program and with the native extension (317), and the hyper-js (215) and hyper-node (334) tests pass.
- A 304 of HY-53 has the headers of the 200 response except `Content-Type`, and HY-52 gives no response without a body a `Content-Type`: a 304 carries no representation (RFC 9110, section 15.4.5), and php-fpm removed the `Content-Type` of a 304 while the Node server sent it, so the two servers answered the same request with other headers. `AppTest` and `app.test.ts` failed before the change.
- Lower the gzip limits of the SSR script and the CSR shell to 31,900 and 33,200 bytes, which the earlier merge had raised to 32,500 and 33,800, and state the rule in the development document: a limit is the measured size plus about 1 %, rounded up to 100 bytes, and a change that grows or shrinks an output past it changes the limit in the same change.
- Remove the data model check of the PHP server before it renders a document. Rendering binds every value of the regions and of the embedded response, so a value outside the data model already fails the document there; the check repeated that work, which costs about 2.3 ms for 1,000 rows. JSON responses, which are not rendered, keep the check (HY-44). A test pins that shared data outside the data model fails a document and JSON.
- The browser no longer checks the manifest that its bundle contains; `createApplication` does not call `checkManifest`. The asset build checks the manifest before it writes anything, and the Node server calls `checkManifest` when it starts, as the PHP server does (HY-2). The check was a repeat of the build and server checks and made up 471 of the SSR script's gzip bytes; a test pins that the asset build rejects an invalid manifest and writes nothing.
- The template repository now requires a namespace for a generated PHP program and checks every number by value. The server build writes the generated program in the namespace `Polyspec\Hyper\Program`, and a number outside ±(2^53 − 1), integer or not, fails a request with 500 and is not a kept value (HY-38, HY-44).
- The build now takes the namespace of the generated PHP program from the application (`--php-namespace`; `Polyspec\Hyper\Examples\Board\Program` for the board) and records it in `program.json`, instead of the fixed `Polyspec\Hyper\Program`, so the programs of several applications load in one PHP process and the rule that a process loads one generated program is removed (HY-48). `make template` also reinstalls the Composer copies of the PHP template package, and tests, assets and server builds run it first: Composer kept a stale copy when the package version did not change. `make check` starts with `make template-check`, which fails when a copy differs from the template repository.
- Remove the attribute `hy-region` and the request header `Hy-Region`, which repeated what the manifest already says. A POST with a non-page `Hy-Region` ran its action (fixed earlier by the rule HY-16, now removed), and an element with a region `id` but without the attribute made htmx put a whole HTML document into the region. A region element is now the element whose `id` is the region name (HY-3, HY-36); the extension requests JSON when the target is the page region element, and the server recognizes a region request by `HX-Request`, which htmx sends (HY-15, HY-21). `make templates-check` checks every region placement of the layout and of the route templates, and region placements take no block arguments.
- On HTTPS the kept cookie is `__Host-hy-keep`, so a sibling subdomain cannot set kept values (HY-39).
- Remove the Chromium `<thead>` measurement from `make bench-browser`; it measured a browser behavior, not this project.
- The PHP server renders with a program that `make server` compiles from the templates: the native template extension when PHP has loaded it, and otherwise the generated PHP program (HY-48). The PHP AST interpreter took 15.7 ms for 1,000 rows on an idle machine, and the generated program 9.6 ms on the same input, with identical output. The native extension was measured only on a heavily loaded machine, so its speed is not recorded here. The server renders every region alone and passes HTML definitions, because a generated program fixes the template of each definition. `make test-php` and `make parity` run with both programs. `App::open` takes `program:` in place of `templates:`, and `TemplateLoader` is removed.
- A JSON response carries kept values apart from the region data (HY-17). `regions` holds the loader data, and `kept` holds the conforming `server` and `cookie` values. The server applies them to render a document, and the browser applies them with its own values. The browser can therefore render a region without kept values that break it (HY-38).
- The server no longer checks cookies for UTF-8 (HY-42). It ignores an invalid `hy-keep`, and starts a new session for an invalid session cookie, instead of answering 400 to every request until the cookie expires.
- The recommended deployment serves direct requests with SSR and htmx requests with JSON from one server; the static shell is for backends that serve JSON only (`docs/operations/deployment.md`).

### Added

- Measure performance with `make bench` (`docs/operations/benchmark.md`). `make bench-server` times `App::handle` per request kind and the rendering, data model and JSON costs for 10 to 1,000 rows. `make bench-browser` measures first screens, region navigation, one `hy-set` change by phase (hyper, template engine, parsing, htmx morph, `htmx.process`) with its main thread load and the parts of every task over 50 ms, memory over 200 navigation cycles with the objects that grew between two heap snapshots and their retainers, and the `blink::MediaQuerySet` objects that Chromium keeps for inserted `<thead>` elements. On 2026-10-01 a `hy-set` change of 1,000 rows took 22 to 25 ms, of which the htmx morph took 13 to 15 ms and the template engine 3 ms; DOM nodes and event listeners stayed constant over 200 cycles.
- Keep region data across reloads (HY-37 to HY-41). A region declares kept paths with their storage: `server` (the server session, saved by a background request that rendering does not wait for), `cookie` (the `hy-keep` cookie written by the browser code), `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`. The server applies `server` and `cookie` values before it renders, so the first SSR document shows them; the browser applies `localStorage` and `sessionStorage` values before it renders a response, and after loading for a server document. A kept value replaces the loader value only at an existing path whose value has the same shape (HY-38); `conformance/keep.json` holds the cases that PHP and JavaScript both pass. The shared data now always contains `csrf`.
- Use all four kinds in the board example: closing the notice (`server`), sorting (`localStorage`), the compact list (`sessionStorage`) and large text on the post page (`cookie`).

- Control the screen with data (HY-29 to HY-36). A route declares route regions that its template places with `{# name}`; each has its own loader. The layout embeds the document JSON with `{# data}` through the reserved template `hyper/data.tpl`, so the first SSR page holds its data. The browser code provides `data`, `render` and `set`, and an element with `hy-set="path=value"` changes the data of its region and renders the region again without a request. There is no change tracking.
- Deliver templates per route (HY-34, HY-35). The asset build writes one hashed AST file per template and an index; the client bundle contains the index only. A region request loads the templates of its route at the same time as the data, and a redirect loads the templates of the final route.
- Add the notice and list route regions to the board example: closing the notice and sorting the list change region data without a request. Define HC-7 and limit a template file to 4,096 gzip bytes.

- Define the screen composition rules (HC-1 to HC-6) in `docs/spec/composition.md`: HTML and CSS for parts that differ only in content, blocks for parts that receive data, regions for parts that update independently, and `hx-*` attributes only in the layout. Add `make templates-check` for HC-6. Verified on 2026-10-01: `make templates-check` passed on the seven board templates and failed on a page template with an added `hx-get` attribute.
- Define the region protocol (HY-1 to HY-28) in `docs/spec/protocol.md`: the application manifest, routing, rendering, requests, JSON responses, the browser code, actions and errors.
- Declare the board layout, title, regions and routes in one manifest, `examples/board/app/app.json`, that PHP and the browser read.
- Add the PHP router and the browser router, and `conformance/routes.json`, the router cases that both pass.
- Add `@polyspec/hyper`, the browser code: the htmx 4 extension that requests JSON for targets marked `hy-region`, routes the response URL, requires the server route to equal the browser route and renders regions; document rendering; and client-side rendering from a static shell with a base path.
- Add `polyspec/hyper`, the PHP server: documents, document JSON, region JSON, 303 redirects for successful actions, 422 pages for rejected input, region selection by changed topics, a base path, and 404 for missing resources.
- Add the board example with list, detail and create pages, server validation, a no-JavaScript fallback, a single-file CSR shell, an edge server that reproduces the CDN behavior, and a comparison page that shows SSR and CSR in two frames.
- Add the asset build, the parity check, the bundle size check, the document check, the deployment document and the end-to-end tests.
